For he knew that she would find it hard to believe; and that if she did believe it, she would be terrified to think that she had married one of the genii, and if not, she would suspect him of a hidden purpose in telling her an empty fable, and he would then be further from her love than before. He held his peace, therefore, for some time, while she watched him, playing with her beads. In reality she was very curious to know the truth, though she had always been unwilling to ask it of him, seeing that she had married him as a stranger, of her own will and choice, without inquiry.

“Is it just,” she asked at last, “that the people should accuse you of evil deeds and fill the air of the city with falsehoods concerning you, so that the very slaves hear the guards repeating the lies to each other in the courtyard, and that I, who am your wife, should not know the truth? What have I done that you should not trust me? Or what have I said that you should regard me no more than a slave who sprinkles the floor and makes the fire, and while she is present in the room you hold your peace lest she should know your thoughts and betray them? Am I not your wife, and faithful? Have I not given you a kingdom and treasure beyond counting? Surely there were times when you talked more freely with that barbarian slave-woman, whose hair was red, than you ever talk with me.”

===Chinese===
“我看出来了，你也想知道，”哈立德说，好奇地望着泽霍瓦，“可我若告诉了你，我想你也不会信，同他们一个样。”

这回轮到泽霍瓦打量他了，可他读不懂她眼睛里的言语。

“你的这个秘密究竟是什么？”她问，“我实在愿意听一听。你若凭安拉向我起誓那是真的，我就信你。因为你是个极其诚实的人，不会绕弯子。”

这话却叫哈立德为难了。他知道她会很难相信；倘若信了，想到自己嫁的是精灵中的一个，她会吓得魂不附体；倘若不信，又会疑心他讲一段空空洞洞的寓言另藏着心思，那他就离她的爱比先前更远了。于是他沉默了好一阵子，她则一面拨弄念珠，一面盯着他。其实她极想知道真相，只是一向不肯问他，因为她当初是凭自己的心意和选择，把这个人当外乡人嫁了，没有盘问过来历。

“这公平吗，”她终于又开口，“百姓诬指你行为邪恶，满城的空气里灌满了关于你的谎言，连奴隶们都听见卫兵在院子里互相复述那些谎话，而我，身为你的妻子，倒不该知道真相？我做了什么事，叫你不肯信任我？还是我说了什么话，叫你把我看得连一个洒地扫房、生火伺候的女奴都不如——她在屋里的时候你就闭口不言，生怕她猜着你的心思、转身出卖你？我不是你的妻子么？不忠实么？我不曾给过你一个王国、数也数不清的财宝么？说起来，你从前同那个红头发的蛮邦女奴说话，倒有几次比同我说的随便多啦。”

===Original===
“This is not true,” said Khaled. “And if I talked familiarly with Almasta, you know the reason, for you yourself found it out, and called me simple for trying to deceive you. And now she is gone to the desert with her husband and there is no more question of her, or her red hair. But all the rest is true, and you have indeed given me a kingdom, which I am likely to lose and wealth which I do not desire, though you have not given me that which I covet more than gold or kingdoms, for I desire it indeed, and that is your love. Moreover if you have given me the rest, I have done something in return, for I have fought for your people, and shed my blood freely, and given you a nation captive, besides loving you and refusing to take another wife into my house. And this last is a matter of which some women would think more highly than you.”

But Zehowah’s curiosity was burning within her like a thirst, for although she had at first cared little to know of Khaled’s former life, she was astonished at his persistency in keeping the secret now, seeing that the whole country was full of false rumours about him.

“How can a man expect that a woman should love him, if he will not put his trust in her?” she asked.

Then Khaled did not hesitate any longer, for he was never slow to do anything by which there seemed to be any hope of gaining her love. He therefore took her hand in his, and it trembled a little so that he was pleased, though indeed the unsteadiness came more from her anxiety to know the story he was about to tell, than from any love she felt at that moment.

===Chinese===
“这不是实话，”哈立德说，“我同阿尔玛斯塔说话随便，缘故你是知道的，因为是你自己识破的，还笑我想瞒你，说我头脑简单。如今她随她丈夫到沙漠里去了，她的事，还有她的红头发，都不必再提。其余的倒是真的：你确实给了我一个王国——眼看我要失掉它了——还给了我不稀罕的财富；可你还没有给我那件比金子和王国更令我渴求的东西，因为我是真心渴求，那就是你的爱。再说，你既给了我这一切，我也回敬了一些：我为你的人民打过仗，毫不吝惜地流过血，把一个国家俘虏了献给你，此外我还爱着你，拒不把第二个妻子娶进家门。这最后一条，有些女人可要比你看得重些。”

可是泽霍瓦的好奇心像干渴一般在她心里烧着。起初她本不大在意哈立德从前的身世，如今全国上下到处是关于他的谣言，他却还死死守着这个秘密，这让她惊诧不已。

“一个男人若不肯信任一个女人，又怎么能指望她爱他呢？”她问。

哈立德便不再迟疑，因为凡是有一线希望赢得她的爱的事，他从不迟慢。于是他把她的手握在自己手里，那只手微微发颤，令他心中欢喜，虽说这点颤动其实多半出于她急于听他将要讲的故事，而不是出于她此刻怀着什么爱意。

===Original===
“You have sworn that you will believe me, Zehowah,” he said. “But I forewarn you that there are hard things to understand. For the reason why I will not tell my father’s name, nor the name of my tribe is a plain one, seeing that I was not born like other men, and have no father at all, and my brethren are not men but genii of the air, created from the beginning and destined to die at the second blast of the trumpet before the resurrection of the dead.”

At this Zehowah started suddenly in fright and looked into his face, expecting to see that he had coals of fire for eyes and an appalling countenance. But when she saw that he was not changed and had the face of a man and the eyes of a man, she laughed.

“What is this idle tale of Afreets?” she exclaimed. “Frighten children with it.”

===Chinese===
“你已经起了誓，说会相信我，泽霍瓦，”他说，“但我先提醒你，有些事情很难懂。我不肯说我父亲的名字，也不说我部落的名字，缘故很简单：我不是像别人那样生下来的，我根本没有父亲；我的兄弟们也不是人，而是空气中的精灵，自太初被造，注定要在死人复活之前、号角第二次吹响的时候死去。”

泽霍瓦听了吓得猛地一惊，直盯着他的脸，只道会看见他两眼如烧红的炭火，面容狰狞可怖。可她看他毫无变化，仍是人的脸、人的眼睛，便笑了。

“这是什么埃弗里特（Afreet）妖怪的闲话？”她叫道，“拿去吓唬小孩子吧。”

===Original===
“This is what I foresaw in you,” said Khaled. “You cannot believe me. Of what use is it then to tell you my story?”

Zehowah answered nothing, for she was angry, supposing that Khaled was attempting to put her off with a foolish tale. She had heard, indeed, of Genii and Afreets and she was sure that they had existence, since they were expressly mentioned in the Koran, but she had never heard that any of them had taken the shape and manner of a man. She remembered also how Khaled had always fought with his hands in war, like other men and been wounded, and she was sure that if his story were true he would have summoned whole legions of his fellows through the air to destroy the enemy.

“You do not believe me,” he repeated somewhat bitterly. “And if you do not believe me, how shall others do so?”

“You ask me to believe too much. If you ask for my faith, you must offer me truths and not fables. It is true that I am curious, which is foolish and womanly. But if you do not wish to tell me your secret, I cannot force you to do so, nor have I any right to expect confidence. Let us therefore talk of other things, or else not talk at all, for though you will not satisfy me you cannot deceive me in this way.”

===Chinese===
“我早就料到你会这样，”哈立德说，“你没法相信我。那我把身世讲给你听，又有什么用处？”

泽霍瓦没有作声，因为她生气了，只当哈立德想拿一个蠢故事搪塞她。她确实听说过精灵和埃弗里特，也确信它们存在，因为《古兰经》里明明白白提到过；可她从没听说它们有哪一个变作过人的形貌、人的举止。她还想起哈立德打仗时向来同别人一样凭双手厮杀，也受过伤；她确信，他的故事若是真的，他早该从空中召来整队整队的同类，把敌人消灭干净了。

“你不信我，”他又说了一遍，带着几分苦涩，“你若不信我，别人又怎么会信？”

“你要求我信的太多了。你要我拿出信心，就得给我真话，而不是寓言。不错，我是好奇，好奇是愚蠢的，是女人气的。可你若不愿把秘密告诉我，我也无法强求，更没有权利指望你交心。那我们还是谈点别的，要么干脆什么都别谈，因为你虽然满足不了我，却也休想这样骗过我。”

===Original===
“So you also believe that I am a Persian and a robber,” said Khaled. “Is it not so?”

“How can I tell what you are, if you will not tell me? Is your name written in your face that I may know it is indeed Khaled and not Ali Hassan as the people say? Or is the record of your deeds inscribed upon your forehead for me to read? You may be a Persian. I cannot tell.”

Then Khaled bent his brows and turned his eyes away from her, for he was angry and disappointed, though indeed she knew in her heart that he was no Persian. But she let him suppose that she thought so, hoping perhaps to goad him into satisfying her curiosity.

If Khaled had been a man like other men, as Zehowah supposed him to be, he would doubtless have invented a well-framed history such as she would have believed, at least for the present. But to him such a falsehood appeared useless, for he had seen the world during many ages and had observed that a lie is never really successful except by chance, seeing that no intelligence is profound enough to foresee the manner in which it will be some day examined, whereas the truth, being always coincident with the reality, can never be wholly refuted.

Khaled therefore hesitated as to whether he should tell his story from the beginning, or hold his peace; but in the end he decided to speak, because it was intolerable to him to be thought an evildoer by her.

===Chinese===
“这么说，你也信我是波斯人、是强盗了，”哈立德说，“是不是这样？”

“你不肯告诉我，我怎么知道你是什么人？你的名字难道写在脸上，好叫我知道你当真是哈立德，而不是百姓说的阿里·哈桑？或是你的行为都刻在额头上，供我去读？你也许真是个波斯人。我说不好。”

哈立德听了蹙起眉头，把目光从她身上移开，他又气又失望，尽管她心里其实明白他不是波斯人。可她任由他这么想，或许是盼着激他一激，好叫他满足她的好奇心。

倘若哈立德真像泽霍瓦以为的那样，是个同众人一样的凡人，他无疑会编造一段编得妥帖的身世，让她信以为真，至少暂时信了。可在他看来，这样的谎言毫无用处，因为他历经许多世代、阅尽人间，看明白了一个道理：谎言若非碰巧，从不曾真正得逞，因为再深的智谋也料不到它有朝一日会被怎样查验；而真话既然永远与事实相合，便永远不会被彻底驳倒。

哈立德于是踌躇起来：是从头把自己的故事讲出来，还是保持沉默；但末了他还是决定开口，因为被她当作恶人，是他断然受不住的。

===Original===
“You make haste to disbelieve, before you have heard all,” he said at last. “Hear me to the end. I have told you that I slew the Indian prince. That was before I became a man. You yourself could not understand how I was able to enter the palace and carry him away without being observed. But as I was at that time able to fly and to make both myself and him invisible, this need not surprise you. If you do not believe that I did it, let us order a litter to be brought for you, and I will take my mare and a sufficient number of attendants, and let us ride southwards into the Red Desert. There I will show you the man’s bones. You will probably recognise them by the gold chain which he wore about his neck and by his ring. After that, when I had buried him, the messenger of Allah came to me, and because the man was an unbeliever, and had intended to embrace the faith outwardly, having evil in his heart, Allah did not destroy me immediately, but commanded that the angel Asrael should write my name in the book of life, that I might become a man. But Allah gave me no soul, promising only that if I could win your love, whose suitor I had killed, I should receive an immortal spirit, which should then be judged according to my deeds. This is truth. I swear it in the name of Allah, the merciful, the compassionate. Then an angel gave me garments such as men wear, and a sword, and a good mare, and I travelled hither to Riad, eating locusts for food. And though no man knew me, you married me at once, for it was the will of Allah, whose will shall also be done to the end. The rest you know. If, therefore, you will love me before I die, I shall receive a soul and it may be that I shall inherit paradise, for I am a true believer and have shed blood for the faith. But if you do not love me, when I die I shall perish as the flame of a lamp that is blown out at dawn. This is the truth.”

He ceased from speaking and looked again at Zehowah. At first he supposed from her face that she believed him, and his heart was comforted, but presently she smiled, and he understood that she was not convinced. For the story had interested her greatly and she had almost forgotten not to believe it, but when she no longer heard his voice, it seemed too hard for her.

“This is a strange tale,” she said, “and it will probably not satisfy the people.”

===Chinese===
“你还没听完，就急着不信了，”他终于说道，“听我讲到底。我告诉过你，是我杀了那个印度王子。那是在我成人之前。你自己也想不透，我怎能进得王宫、把他劫走而不被人察觉。可我那时既能飞行，又能叫我自己和他都隐去身形，这就不必奇怪了。你若不信是我干的，咱们就叫人给你备一乘肩舆，我带上我的母马和足够的随从，一同往南骑进红沙漠去。我带你去看那人的骸骨。你多半认得出来，凭他脖子上挂的金链，还有他的戒指。在那之后，我埋葬了他，安拉的使者便临到我这里。因为那人是个不信者，原打算表面上皈依正教、心里却藏着恶念，安拉没有立刻毁灭我，而是吩咐天使阿兹拉尔把我的名字写进生命之书，叫我可以成为人。但安拉没有给我灵魂，只应许说，我若能赢得那被我杀了求婚者的女子的爱，便可领受一个不死的灵魂，日后依我的行为受审判。这是真话。我凭安拉之名起誓，那慈悲者、怜悯者之名。随后一位天使给了我世人穿的衣服、一把剑和一匹好母马，我便一路行到里雅得来，靠蝗虫充饥。虽然没有一个人认得我，你却当即嫁了我，因为那是安拉的旨意，这旨意也必将行到最后。其余的事你都知道了。所以，你若肯在我死之前爱我，我就得着一个灵魂，说不定还能承受天堂，因为我是真信士，曾为信仰流过血。你若不爱我，我死的时候，就要像黎明时被一口吹灭的灯火一样消散。这就是真相。”

他住了口，又望着泽霍瓦。起初他看她的脸色，还以为她信了，心里得了安慰；可不多时她微微一笑，他便明白她并没有被说服。因为这故事让她听得着了迷，几乎忘了不去信它；可他的声音一停，她又觉得这实在难以相信。

“这是个奇怪的故事，”她说，“只怕它难以让百姓心服。”

===Original===
“I do not care whether they are satisfied or not,” Khaled answered. “All I desire is to be believed by you, for I cannot bear that you should think me what I am not.”

“What can I do? I cannot say to my intelligence, take this and reject that, any more than I can say to my heart, love or love not. It would indeed have been easier if you had said, ‘I am a certain Persian, a fugitive, protect me, for my enemies are upon me.’ I could perhaps give you protection if you require it, as you may. But you come to me with a monstrous tale, and you ask me to love, not a man, but a Djinn or an Afreet, or whatever it pleases you to call yourself. Assuredly this is too hard for me.”

And again Zehowah smiled scornfully, for she was really beginning to think that he might be a Persian disguised as the people said.

“I need no protection from man or woman,” said Khaled, “for I fear neither the one nor the other. For I am strong, and if I am able to give out of charity I am also able to take by force. My fate is ever with me. I cannot escape it. But neither can others escape theirs. I will fight alone if need be, for if you will not love me I care little how I may end. Moreover, in battle, it is not good to stand in the way of a man who seeks death.”

But Zehowah thought this might be the speech of a desperate man such as Ali Hassan, the robber, as well as of Khaled, the Djinn, and she was not convinced, though she no longer smiled. For she knew little of supernatural beings, and a devil might easily call himself a good spirit, so that she was convinced that she was married either to a demon or to a dangerous robber, and she could not even decide which of the two she would have preferred, for either was bad enough, and as for love there could no longer be any question of that.

===Chinese===
“他们心服不服，与我何干，”哈立德答道，“我一心只愿你能相信，因为我受不住你把我当成另一个我不是的人。”

“我能怎么样？我没法对我的理智说，收下这个、扔掉那个，正如我没法对我的心说，爱或者不爱。你当初若说：‘我是某家的波斯人，是个逃亡的人，保护我吧，我的仇人追上来了。’那倒容易些。你若需要保护，我或许还给得起，你也可能真需要。可你带来的是一个骇人听闻的故事，你还要我去爱的不是一个人，而是一个巨灵（Djinn）、一个埃弗里特，或是随你高兴怎么称呼自己的什么。这实在是太难为我了。”

泽霍瓦又轻蔑地笑了，因为她当真开始觉得，他也许正如百姓所说，是个改头换面的波斯人。

“我既不需要男人的保护，也不需要女人的，”哈立德说，“因为我谁也不怕。我有力量，既能仗义施舍，也能凭强力夺取。我的命运始终随我而行，我逃不脱它，别人也逃不脱他们的。必要时我可以独自迎战，因为你若不爱我，我落个什么下场都无关紧要。再说，在战场上，拦住一个一心寻死的人的去路，可不是好事。”

可泽霍瓦心想，这番话与其说出自哈立德这个巨灵，不如说更像阿里·哈桑那号亡命强盗的口吻，她仍没有被说服，虽然她不再笑了。她对超凡的存在所知甚少，魔鬼也很容易自称善灵，于是她认定自己嫁的不是恶魔，便是危险的强盗，甚至说不清这两者她更情愿哪一样，因为哪一样都够糟了；至于爱，那更是再无从谈起。

===Original===
Khaled understood well enough and rose from his seat and went away, desiring to be alone. He knew that he was now surrounded by danger on every side and that he could not even look to his wife for comfort, since she also believed him to be an impostor.

“Truly,” he said to himself, “this is a task beyond accomplishment, which Allah has laid upon me. It is harder to get a woman’s love than to win kingdoms, and it is easier to destroy a whole army with one stroke of a sword than to make a woman believe that which she does not desire. And now the end is at hand. For she will never love me and I shall certainly perish in this fight, being alone against so many. Allah assuredly did not intend me to run away, and moreover there is no reason left for remaining alive.”

On that day Khaled again called the chief men together in his *kahwah*, and addressed them briefly.

“Men of Riad,” he said, “I am aware that there is a conspiracy to overthrow and destroy me, and I daresay that you yourselves are among the plotters. I will not tell you who I am, but I swear by Allah that I am neither a Persian nor a robber, nor yet a Shiyah. You will doubtless attack me unawares, but you will not find me sleeping. I will kill as many of you as I can, and afterwards I also shall undoubtedly be killed, for I am alone and you have many thousands on your side. *Min Allah*﻿—it is in Allah’s hands. Go in peace.”

So they departed, shaking their heads, but saying nothing.

===Chinese===
哈立德心里全明白了。他起身离座，走了出去，想独自待着。他知道此刻四面八方都被危险围住，连妻子那里也讨不到安慰，因为她也把他当成了骗子。

“真的，”他自言自语道，“安拉加在我身上的，是一件办不成的差事。得一个女人的爱，比征服王国还难；一剑扫荡一支大军，也比叫一个女人相信她不愿相信的事容易。如今结局近了。她永远不会爱我，这一场争斗里我孤身对抗众人，必死无疑。安拉自然不是造我来逃跑的，再说，活下去也已没有理由了。”

当天，哈立德又把头人们召到*卡赫瓦*厅里，简短地讲了话。

“里雅得的众人，”他说，“我知道有一桩阴谋，要推翻我、毁灭我，而且我敢说，你们自己就在同谋之列。我不会告诉你们我是谁，但我凭安拉起誓，我既不是波斯人，也不是强盗，更不是什叶派。你们多半会乘我不备下手，可你们不会发现我在酣睡。我能杀你们多少人，便杀多少，随后我自己无疑也会被杀，因为我孤身一人，你们那边却有成千上万。*Min Allah*﻿——一切都在安拉手中。平安地去吧。”

于是他们摇着头走了，一句话也没有说。

## V / 五

===Original===
When Khaled was within half a day’s march of Riad, the Sultan came out to meet him with a great train of attendants and courtiers, with cooks bringing food and sweetmeats, and a number of musicians. And they all encamped together for a short time in the shade of the trees, for there were gardens in the place. The Sultan embraced Khaled and put upon him a very magnificent garment, after which they sat down together in a large tent which the Sultan had brought with him. When they had eaten and refreshed themselves they began to talk, and Khaled told his father-in-law all that he had done, and gave him an account of the spoils which he had brought back, commanding the most valuable objects to be brought into the tent. After this the Sultan desired to see the women captives.

“There is one especially whom it may please you to take for yourself,” said Khaled, and he ordered Almasta to be brought in.

When the male slaves had left the tent, Almasta drew aside her veil. The Sultan looked at her and smiled, stroking his beard, for he was much pleased.

“Her face is like a pearl and her hair is a setting of red gold,” he said. “Truly she is like the sunrise on a fair morning when there are red clouds in the east.”

===Chinese===
哈立德行至距里雅得（Riad）只有半日路程时，苏丹率大批侍从与朝臣出城相迎，厨役抬着肴馔与甜食，乐师也来了许多。众人便在树荫下暂时扎营歇息，因为那地方素来多园圃。苏丹拥抱了哈立德，将一件极华美的袍服披在他身上，随后二人一同在苏丹带来的一顶大帐中坐定。饮食既毕，精神复振，他们便叙谈起来。哈立德把自己此番所为原原本本告诉了岳父，又将带回的战利品逐一禀报，命人把最贵重之物抬进帐中。之后，苏丹提出要见一见被俘的女子。

“其中有一个，您若取来自己受用，想必会称心，”哈立德说着，便吩咐把阿尔玛斯塔（Almasta）带进来。

男奴们退出帐后，阿尔玛斯塔揭开了面纱。苏丹望着她，捋着胡须微笑，心里十分欢喜。

“她的脸像一颗珍珠，头发像红金的镶嵌，”他说，“真像晴朗清晨的日出，东方正铺着红霞。”

===Original===
Almasta looked attentively at him, and afterwards she glanced at Khaled, who could not avoid looking at her on account of her beauty. Her face was grave and indifferent. Then Khaled told the Sultan how she had hated the Sultan of Shammar and had tried to kill him on the journey.

“This is a dangerous woman, my son,” said the old man. But he laughed as he said it, for although he was old, he was no coward. “She is dangerous, indeed. Will you love me, pearl of my soul’s treasures?” he inquired of her, still smiling.

“You are my lord and my master,” she answered, looking down.

When Khaled heard this he wondered whether his father-in-law would get any affection from her. Zehowah had answered in the same words.

===Chinese===
阿尔玛斯塔凝神打量他，随后又瞥了哈立德一眼——她的美貌使哈立德不由自主地看着她。她面色沉静而淡漠。于是哈立德告诉苏丹，她如何憎恨沙玛尔（Shammar）的苏丹，如何在旅途中设法要杀他。

“这是个危险的女人，我的儿，”老人说。但他一边说一边笑，因为他人虽老，却不是懦夫。“她确实危险。你肯爱我吗，我灵魂宝库中的明珠？”他仍旧笑着问她。

“您是我的主人和君王，”她垂目答道。

哈立德听了这话，心里疑惑岳父能否从她那里得到半点真情。当初泽霍瓦答的也是同样的话。

===Original===
“By Allah, I will give you such gifts as will make you love me,” said the Sultan. “What shall I give you?”

“His head,” answered Almasta, raising her eyes quickly.

“The head of the Sultan of Shammar?”

Almasta nodded, and Khaled could see that her lips trembled.

===Chinese===
“以安拉（Allah）起誓，我要送你礼物，送得你爱上我，”苏丹说，“你要什么？”

“他的头颅，”阿尔玛斯塔答道，倏地抬起眼睛。

“沙玛尔苏丹的头颅？”

阿尔玛斯塔点了点头，哈立德看得出她的嘴唇在颤抖。

===Original===
“A dead man has no companions,” said the Sultan, looking at Khaled to see what he would do. But Khaled cared little, and said nothing.

So the Sultan called a slave and ordered the captive’s head to be struck off immediately. Then Almasta threw herself upon the carpet on the floor of the tent and embraced his feet.

“See how easily the love of a woman is got,” Khaled thought, “even by an old man whose beard is grey and his limbs heavy.”

When Almasta rose again, she looked at Khaled triumphantly, as though to remind him of the night on the journey when he had hindered her from killing the captive in his cage. But though he understood her, he held his peace, for he had cared nothing whether the prisoner lived or died after he had delivered him over to his father-in-law, and he was considering whether he might not please Zehowah in some similar manner. This was not easy, however, for he was not aware that Zehowah had any private enemy, whose head he might offer her.

===Chinese===
“死人是不需要伴侣的，”苏丹说着，看看哈立德，想瞧他有何举动。哈立德却毫不在意，一言不发。

于是苏丹唤来一名奴隶，下令立即斩下那俘虏的头颅。阿尔玛斯塔随即扑倒在地毯上，抱住他的双脚。

“瞧，女人的爱多么容易到手，”哈立德心想，“连一个须发苍苍、四肢沉重的老头子也能得到。”

阿尔玛斯塔再站起来时，得意扬扬地望着哈立德，仿佛要提醒他旅途中那个夜晚——那时他拦阻她，不让她杀那笼中的俘虏。哈立德虽然懂得她的意思，却缄口不言，因为他把俘虏交给岳父之后，这人是死是活他全不放在心上；此刻他心里盘算的，是能否用类似的法子也讨泽霍瓦的欢心。然而这并不容易，因为他想不出泽霍瓦有什么私仇，可以献上仇人的头颅。

===Original===
After the Sultan had seen the other women and the best of the spoils, Khaled begged that he might be allowed to ride on into Riad alone, for he saw that the Sultan intended to spend the night in feasting where he had encamped. The Sultan was so much pleased with Almasta and so greatly diverted in examining the rich stuffs and the gold and silver vessels and jewels, that he let Khaled go, almost without trying to detain him, though he made him many speeches praising his conduct of the war, and would have loaded him with gifts. But Khaled would take nothing with him, saying that he would only receive his just share with the rest; and the fame of his generosity immediately went abroad among the soldiers and the Bedouins throughout all the camp.

“For,” said Khaled, “there is not a fleeter mare than mine among all those we have taken; my sword proves to be a good one, for I have tried it well; as for women, I am satisfied with one wife; and besides a wife, a sword and a horse, there are no treasures in the world which I covet.”

===Chinese===
苏丹看过其余的女子和最好的战利品之后，哈立德请求准他独自先行进城，因为他看出苏丹打算就地设宴，欢度一夜。苏丹对阿尔玛斯塔满心欢喜，又忙着鉴赏那些华丽的织物、金银器皿和珠宝，兴致正浓，便放哈立德走了，几乎不曾挽留，只是说了许多称赞他用兵的话，还想把礼物堆到他身上。哈立德却一概不带，说自己只愿同众人一样领受应得的一份。于是他慷慨的名声立刻传遍了全营的士兵和贝都因人（Bedouins）中间。

“因为，”哈立德说，“掳来的马匹里再没有一匹比我的母马更快；我的剑经我好好试过，是把好剑；至于女人，我有一个妻子便满足了。除妻子、剑和马之外，世上再没有我贪图的财宝。”

===Original===
So Khaled rode away alone into Riad, for he desired no company, being busy with his own thoughts. He reached the gates at nightfall and went immediately to the palace and entered Zehowah’s apartments. He found her sitting among her women in her accustomed place, listening to the tales of an old woman who sat in the midst of the circle. As soon as Zehowah saw her husband she sprang up gladly to meet him, as a friend would have done.

“Though it is summertime, I have pursued the enemy,” said Khaled. “And though the sun was hot, I have got the victory and brought home the spoil.”

He said this remembering how she had tried to hinder him from going. Then he gave her his sword and he sat down with her, while the women brought food and drink, for he was weary, and hungry and thirsty. The women also brought their musical instruments and began to sing songs in praise of Khaled’s deeds; but after a time he sent them all away and remained alone with Zehowah.

===Chinese===
于是哈立德独自策马进了里雅得，他不要人随行，只想独自想心事。他黄昏时分抵达城门，径直进宫，走进泽霍瓦的内室。见她照常坐在侍女们中间，听一个坐在圈子中央的老妇讲故事。泽霍瓦一见丈夫归来，欣然起身相迎，就像迎接一位友人。

“虽是盛夏，我仍追击了敌人，”哈立德说，“虽是烈日当空，我仍得了胜，把战利品带回了家。”

他说这话，是想起了她当初如何阻拦他出征。随后他把剑递给她，在她身旁坐下。侍女们端来饮食，因为他又累又饥又渴。侍女们又取来乐器，唱起歌颂哈立德战功的歌；过了一阵，他把她们都打发出去，独自与泽霍瓦相对。

===Original===
“O Zehowah,” he said, “you are my law and my rule. You are my speech and my occupation. You are my *Kebla* to which I turn in prayer. For the love of you I have got the victory over many foes. And yet I see that your cheek is cold and the light of your eyes is undisturbed. Have you no other enemies for me to destroy, or have you no secret foe whose head would be a pleasant gift?”

Zehowah laughed, as she fanned him with a palm leaf.

“Do you still thirst for war, Khaled?” she asked. “Truly you have swallowed up all our enemies as the dry sand swallows up water. Where shall I find enemies enough for you to slay? You went out in pride and you have returned in glory. Are you not yet satisfied? And as for any secret foe, if I have any I do not know him. Rest, therefore; eat and drink and spend your days in peace.”

===Chinese===
“泽霍瓦啊，”他说，“你是我的法度，我的纲纪。你是我的言语，我的事业。你是我的*基布拉*（Kebla），我祈祷时朝向的方位。为了爱你，我战胜了许多仇敌。可我仍见你的面颊是冷的，你眼中的光波澜不惊。你还有别的仇敌要我去剪除吗？你可有隐秘的仇人，他的头颅是一份可心的礼物？”

泽霍瓦笑了，用一片棕榈叶替他扇风。

“你还渴望打仗吗，哈立德？”她问，“你当真把我们的仇敌吞灭尽了，就像干沙吞水一样。我到哪里再找够你杀的敌人呢？你意气昂扬地出征，荣荣耀耀地回来。你还不满足吗？至于隐秘的仇人，若有，我也不认识他。所以歇息吧；吃吃喝喝，安安稳稳度日。”

===Original===
“I care little for either food or drink,” Khaled answered, “and I need little rest.”

“Will nothing but war please you? Must you overcome Egypt and make Syria pay tribute as far as Damascus before you will rest?”

“I will conquer the whole world for you, if you wish it,” said Khaled.

“What should I do with the world?” asked Zehowah. “Have I not treasures and garments enough and to spare, besides the spoil you have now brought home? And besides, if you would conquer the world you must needs make war upon true believers, amongst whom we do not count the people of Shammar. Be satisfied therefore and rest in peace.”

===Chinese===
“饮食我都不放在心上，”哈立德答道，“歇息我也用不着多少。”

“除了打仗，什么都不能让你称心？难道非得征服埃及（Egypt），叫叙利亚（Syria）一直纳贡到大马士革（Damascus），你才肯歇息？”

“只要你愿意，我为你征服全世界，”哈立德说。

“我要世界做什么用？”泽霍瓦问，“且不说你这趟带回来的战利品，我的财宝衣裳难道还不够多、还有富余吗？再说，你要征服世界，就免不了向信士们开战，而沙玛尔人是不算在信士之列的。所以知足吧，安享太平。”

===Original===
“How shall I be satisfied until I have kindled the light in Zehowah’s eyes at my coming, and until I feel that her hand is cold and trembles when I take it in mine?”

“Do I say to my eyes, ‘be dull’﻿—or to my hand, ‘do not tremble’?” Zehowah asked. “Is this, which you ask of me, something I can command at will, as I can a smile or a word? If it is, teach me and I will learn. But if not, why do you expect of me what I cannot do? Can a camel gallop like a horse, or a horse trot like a camel, or bear great burdens through the desert? Have you come back from a great war only to talk of this something which you call love, which is yours and not mine, which you feel and I cannot feel, which you cannot explain nor describe, and which, after all, is but a whim of the fancy, as one man loves sour drink and another sweet?”

“Do you think that love is nothing but a whim of the fancy?” asked Khaled bitterly.

“What else can it be? Would you love me if you were blind?”

===Chinese===
“我一来，泽霍瓦眼里便燃起光亮；我握住她的手时，感到那手发凉而颤抖——不到这般光景，我如何满足得了？”

“难道我能对自己的眼睛说‘黯淡下来’，或对手说‘不许颤抖’吗？”泽霍瓦问，“你所求于我的这件事，难道是我能随意支使的，就像支使一个微笑、一句话？若是，你教我，我就学。若不是，你为何指望我做我做不到的事？骆驼能像马一样奔驰吗？马能像骆驼一样缓步，能驮着重负穿越沙漠吗？你打了一场大仗回来，就只为谈这个你所谓的爱？它是你的，不是我的；你感受得到，我感受不到；你既解释不出，也描画不成；说到底，不过是心里的一个念头，就像有人爱酸饮、有人爱甜饮罢了。”

“你以为爱不过是心里的一个念头吗？”哈立德苦涩地问。

“不然还能是什么？你若瞎了，还会爱我吗？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

“And if you were deaf?”

“Yes.”

“And if you could not touch my face with your hands, nor kiss me with your lips?”

“Yes.”

Zehowah laughed.

===Chinese===
“会。”

“若你聋了呢？”

“会。”

“若你的手摸不到我的脸，唇也吻不到我呢？”

“会。”

泽霍瓦笑了。

===Original===
“Then love is indeed a fancy. For if you could not see me, nor touch me, nor hear me, what would remain to you but an empty thought?”

“Have I seen you, or touched you, or heard your voice for these two months and a half?” asked Khaled. “Yet I have loved you as much during all that time.”

“You mean that you have thought of me, as I have thought of you, by the memory of what was not fancy, but reality. Would you dispute with me, Khaled? You will find me subtle.”

===Chinese===
“那么爱果然是个念头。因为你既看不见我，摸不到我，也听不见我，除了一个空空的念头，还能剩下什么？”

“这两个半月里，我可曾见过你、碰过你、听过你的声音？”哈立德问，“然而这段时间里，我对你的爱一分未减。”

“你的意思是，你想念过我，正如我也想念过你，靠的是记忆——记着那并非虚念、而是实有的事。你要同我争辩吗，哈立德？你会发觉我不简单。”

===Original===
“There is more wit in my arm than in my head,” Khaled answered, “and it is not easy for a man to persuade a woman.”

“It is very easy, provided that the man have reason on his side. But where are the treasures you have brought back, the slaves and the rich spoils? I would gladly see some of them, for the messengers you sent told great tales of the riches of Haïl.”

“Tomorrow they will be brought into the city. Your father has remained feasting in the gardens towards Dereyiyah, and the whole army with him. I rode hither alone.”

===Chinese===
“我臂膀里的智慧比脑袋里的多，”哈立德答道，“要男人说服女人，本来就不容易。”

“那很容易，只要男人占着理。可你带回来的财宝在哪儿？奴隶和那些丰厚的战利品呢？我很想看看，因为你派来的信使把哈伊勒（Haïl）的富庶夸得天花乱坠。”

“明天就运进城。你父亲留在往德拉伊耶（Dereyiyah）方向去的园子里饮宴，全军都陪着他。我独自骑马回来的。”
===Original===
“Why did you not remain too?”

“Because that whim of the fancy which I call love brought me back,” Khaled answered.

“Then I am glad you love me,” said Zehowah. “For I am glad you came quickly.”

“Are you truly glad?”

===Chinese===
“你为何不留下来同乐？”

“因为我称之为爱的那个念头，把我带回来了，”哈立德答道。

“那我倒高兴你爱我，”泽霍瓦说，“因为你回来得快，我很高兴。”

“你是真高兴？”

===Original===
“I was very tired of my women,” she answered. “I am sorry you have brought nothing with you. Are there any among the captives who are beautiful?”

“There is one, a present sent lately to the Sultan of Shammar. She is very beautiful, and unlike all the rest. Your father is much pleased with her, and will perhaps marry her.”

“Of what kind is her beauty?” asked Zehowah.

“She is as white as milk, her eyes are twin sapphires, her mouth is a rose, her hair is like gold reddened in fire.”

===Chinese===
“我早就腻烦我的侍女们了，”她答道，“可惜你什么都没带回来。俘虏里可有貌美的？”

“有一个，是新近送给沙玛尔苏丹的礼物。她非常美，与其余的都不同。你父亲很喜欢她，多半会娶她。”

“她的美是怎样的？”泽霍瓦问。

“她白得像奶，眼睛是一对蓝宝石，嘴是一朵玫瑰，头发像火里烧红的金子。”

===Original===
Zehowah was silent for a while, and twisted a string of musk-beads round her fingers.

“The others are all Arabian women,” Khaled said at last.

“Why did you not keep the beautiful one for yourself?” asked Zehowah, suddenly throwing aside her beads and looking at him curiously. “Surely you, who have borne the brunt of the war, might have chosen for yourself what pleased you best.”

Khaled looked at her in great astonishment.

===Chinese===
泽霍瓦沉默了片刻，把一串麝香珠绕在指间。

“其余的都是阿拉伯女子，”哈立德终于说。

“那最美的你为何不自己留下？”泽霍瓦问，忽然把珠串丢在一边，好奇地打量他，“独任这场战事艰危的是你，总该挑件最称心的归自己吧。”

哈立德极为惊讶地望着她。

===Original===
“Have I not married Zehowah? Would you have me take another wife?”

“Why not? Is it not lawful for a man to take four wives at one time? And this woman might have loved you, as you desire to be loved.”

“Would it be nothing to you, if I took her?”

“Nothing. I am the King’s daughter. I shall always be first in the house. I say, she might love you. Then you would be satisfied.”

===Chinese===
“我娶的不是泽霍瓦吗？你要我再娶一个？”

“有何不可？一个男人同时娶四房妻室，不是合法的吗？这女子本可以爱你，如你所渴望被爱的那样。”

“我若娶了她，你竟无所谓？”

“无所谓。我是王的女儿，在这个家里永远居首。我说呀，她也许会爱你，那样你就满足了。”

===Original===
“Zehowah, Zehowah!” cried Khaled. “Is love a piece of gold, that it matters not whence it be, so long as a man has it in his own possession? Or is it wood of the ’Ood tree that one may buy it and bring it home and make the whole house fragrant with it? Is a man’s heart like his belly, which is alike satisfied with different kinds of food?”

“He who eats, knows by the taste whether he eats Persian mutton, or barley bread, or only broiled locusts. But a man who believes that he is loved, knows that he is loved, so far as knowing is possible, and must be satisfied, if to be loved is what he desires.”

“That may be true. But he who desires bread is not satisfied with locusts. It is your love which I would have. Not the love of another.”

===Chinese===
“泽霍瓦，泽霍瓦！”哈立德喊道，“难道爱是一块金子，只要握在自己手里，是哪里来的都无所谓？难道它是乌德树（’Ood）的香木，买来带回家，就能满屋生香？难道男人的心像他的肚腹，各样的食物吃下去都一样果腹？”

“吃东西的人，尝得出吃的是波斯羊肉，还是大麦面包，或不过是烤蝗虫。但相信自己被爱的人，只要认知可能，便知道自己被爱；他若所求的就是被爱，就该满足了。”

“这或许是真的。可想吃面包的人，吃蝗虫是不会饱的。我要的是你的爱，不是别人的爱。”

===Original===
“You are like a man who hopes to get by argument a sum of money from one who has nothing,” said Zehowah, smiling at him. “Can you make gold grow in the purse of a beggar? Or can you cause a *ghada* bush to bear dates by reasoning with it? Your heart is a palm tree, but mine is a *ghada* bush.”

“Yet an angel may touch the *ghada* and it will bear fruit,” answered Khaled, for he remembered how the angel had turned dry leaves into rich garments for him to wear.

“Doubtless, Allah can do all things. But where is the angel? Hear me, Khaled, for I speak very reasonably, as a wife should speak to her husband, who is her lord and master. My lord is not satisfied with me and desires something of me which is not mine to give. Let him take another wife beside me. I have given my lord a kingdom and great riches and power. Let him take another wife now, who will give him this fancy of his thoughts for which he yearns, though she have no other possessions. In this way my lord will be satisfied.”

===Chinese===
“你就像一个人，想靠讲理从一个身无分文的人手里讨出一笔钱来，”泽霍瓦含笑望着他，“你能让乞丐的钱袋里长出金子吗？你能同*加达*（ghada）灌木讲道理，叫它结出椰枣吗？你的心是一棵椰枣树，我的心却是一丛加达灌木。”

“然而天使一触*加达*，它就会结果，”哈立德答道，因为他记得天使如何把枯叶变成华服给他穿。

“无疑，安拉无所不能。可天使在哪里？听我说，哈立德，我这话说得在理，正如妻子对丈夫——自己的主人和君王——所当说的。我的主人不满足于我，向我索求一样不属于我、我给不出的东西。那就让他再娶一房吧。我已给了我的主人王国、巨富和权势。让他如今再娶一个，把他在心里渴念的这桩痴想给他，哪怕她再无别样所有。这样，我的主人就满足了。”

===Original===
Khaled listened sadly to what Zehowah said, and he began to despair, for he was not subtle in argument nor eloquent in speech. The reason of this was plain. In the days when he had been one of the genii he had wandered over the whole Earth and had heard the eloquence of all nations and the arguments of all philosophers, learning therefrom that deeds are no part of words, and that they who would be believed must speak little and do much. But the genii possess no insight into the hearts of women.

Khaled reflected also that the length of life granted him was uncertain, and that he had already spent two months and a half at a distance from Zehowah in accomplishing the conquest whereby he had hoped to win her love. But since this had utterly failed, he cast about in his mind for some new deed to do, which could be done without leaving her even for a short time. But he was troubled by her indifference, and most of all by her proposing that he should take another wife. As he thought of this, he was filled with horror, and he understood that he loved Zehowah more than he had supposed, since he could not bear to think of setting another woman beside her.

===Chinese===
哈立德黯然听着泽霍瓦的话，渐渐绝望起来，因为他不擅辞辩，也不善言辞。缘由也明白：他做精灵（genii）的那些年月里，曾游遍大地，听过万国的雄辩、诸哲的论难，由此懂得事迹不在言语之中，欲使人信，须少说多做。然而精灵们窥不透女人的心。

哈立德又想到，自己被准许的寿数渺茫难料，而为了这场指望赢得她爱情的征伐，他已有两个半月远离泽霍瓦。既然这指望全然落空，他便在心里琢磨一件新的功业，一件无须片刻离开她也能做成的事。可她的冷淡使他烦恼，而她劝他再娶一房，尤其使他痛苦。想到这里，他满心惊惧，才明白自己爱泽霍瓦远比料想的深——他连把另一个女子放在她身旁想一想都受不住。

===Original===
Then his face became very dark and his eyes were like camp fires far off in the desert, and he took Zehowah’s wrist in his hand, holding it tightly as though he would not let it go. As his heart grew hot in his breast, words came to his lips unawares like the speech of a man in a dream, and he heard his own voice as it were from a distance.

“I will not take another,” he said. “What is the love of any other woman to me? It is as dust in the throat of a man thirsting for water. Show me a woman who loves me. Her face shall be but a cold mirror in which the image of a fire is reflected without warmth, her soft words shall be to me as the screaming of a parrot, her touch a thorn and her lips ashes. What is it to me if all the women of the world love me? Kindle a fire and burn them before me, for I care not. Let them perish all together, for I shall not know that they are gone. I love you and not another. Shall it profit a man to fill his mouth with dust, though it be the dust of gold mingled with precious stones, when he desires water? Or shall he be warmed in winter by the reflection of a fire in a mirror? By Allah! I want neither the wealth of Haïl, nor a wife with red hair. Let them take gold who do not ask for love. I want but one thing, and Zehowah alone can give it to me. *Wallah!* My heart burns. But I would give it to be burned forever in hell if I might get your love now. This I ask. This only I desire. For this I will suffer and for this I am ready to die before my time.”

===Chinese===
他的脸色变得阴沉，双眼像沙漠远处的篝火。他握住泽霍瓦的手腕，紧紧攥着，仿佛再不肯放开。胸中火热起来，话便不觉到了唇边，像梦中之人的言语，他听着自己的声音，仿佛来自远方。

“我不要再娶，”他说，“别的女人的爱于我算什么？那不过是干渴之人喉咙里的尘土。给我看一个爱我的女人吧。她的脸不过是一面冰冷的镜子，映着火的影像却没有温度；她的软语于我不过是鹦鹉的聒叫；她的抚摸是荆棘，她的唇是灰烬。全世界的女人都爱我，于我又有什么相干？点火来，把她们烧给我看，我不在乎。让她们一起死绝好了，因为她们死了我也不会察觉。我爱的是你，不是别人。一个人渴的时候，嘴里塞满尘土——纵是混着宝石的金尘——能顶什么用？冬天里对着镜中映出的火，能暖得了身吗？安拉作证！哈伊勒的财富我不要，红头发的妻室我也不要。让不求爱情的人拿金子去吧。我只要一样东西，只有泽霍瓦能给我。*瓦拉*！（Wallah！）我的心在燃烧。可若此刻能得到你的爱，我甘愿把它扔进地狱里永世焚烧。这就是我所求的。这是我唯一想要的。为此我甘受苦难，为此我随时准备死在我的寿数之前。”

===Original===
Zehowah was silent, looking at him with wonder, and yet not altogether pleased. She saw that she could not understand him, though she did as well as she could.

“Has he not all that the heart of man can desire?” she thought. “Am I not young and beautiful, and possessed of many jewels and treasures? Have I not given him wealth and power, and has he not with his own hand got the victory over his enemies and mine? And yet he is not satisfied. Surely, he is too hard to please.”

But he, reading her thoughts from her face, continued in his speech.

===Chinese===
泽霍瓦沉默了，惊奇地望着他，却又不全然欢喜。她看得出自己无法懂得他，尽管她已尽了力。

“凡人心所渴望的，他不都齐备了吗？”她想，“我难道不年轻貌美，没有许多珠宝财货？我给了他财富和权势，他又亲手战胜了他和我的仇敌。可他还不满足。诚然，他太难讨好了。”

但他从她脸上读出了心事，继续说道。

===Original===
“What is all the happiness of the world without love?” he asked. “It is like a banquet in which many rich viands are served, but the guests cannot eat them because there is no salt in any of them. And what is a beautiful woman without love? She is like a garden in which there are all kinds of rare flowers, and much grass, and deep shade, but in which a man cannot live, because nothing grows there which he can eat when he is hungry.”

“Truly,” said Zehowah, “that is what you will make of your life. For there is a garden called Irem, planted in a secret place of the deserts about Aden, by Sheddad the son of Ad, who desired to outdo the gardens of paradise, and was destroyed for his impiety with all his people, by the hand of Allah. But a certain man named Abdullah ibn Kelabah was searching in the desert for a lost camel, and came unawares upon this place. There were fruits and water there and all that a man could wish for, and Abdullah dwelt in peace and plenty, praising Allah. Then on a certain day he desired to eat an onion, and finding none anywhere, he went out, intending to obtain one, and having eaten it, to return immediately. But though he searched the desert many months he was never able to find the garden again. Wherefore it is said that Abdullah ibn Kelabah lost the earthly paradise of Irem for a mouthful of onion.”

===Chinese===
“没有爱，世上的种种幸福算什么？”他问，“那好比一场盛筵，珍馐摆了许多，却一样都没放盐，客人们无从下咽。没有爱的美人又算什么？她像一座花园，奇花异卉应有尽有，芳草萋萋，浓荫匝地，人却活不下去，因为他饥饿时，园中长不出一样可吃的东西。”

“真的，”泽霍瓦说，“那你的一生就要变成这样了。传说有一座花园叫伊雷姆（Irem），是阿德（Ad）之子谢达德（Sheddad）在亚丁（Aden）四周沙漠中的一处秘境种下的；他想胜过天国的园囿，却因不敬神而连同他的百姓一起，被安拉亲手毁灭了。后来有个名叫阿卜杜拉·伊本·凯拉白（Abdullah ibn Kelabah）的人在沙漠里寻一头走失的骆驼，无意间闯到了那地方。那里有果子、有水，凡人所愿的一应俱全，阿卜杜拉便安居富足，称颂安拉。可有一天他想吃一头洋葱，四处寻不见，便走出去想弄一个来，吃了就立刻回来。然而他在沙漠里寻了好几个月，却再也没能找到那座花园。所以人们说，阿卜杜拉·伊本·凯拉白为了一口洋葱，丢了他的地上乐园伊雷姆。”

===Original===
“How can you understand me if you do not love me?” asked Khaled. “Love has its own language, and when two love they understand each the other’s words. But when the one loves and the other loves not, they are strangers, though they be man and wife; or they are like Persians and Arabians not understanding either the other’s speech, or that if the wife cries ‘father,’ her husband will bring her a cup of water supposing her to be thirsty. For those who would speak one language must be of one heart, and they who would be of one heart must love each other.”

Then Zehowah sighed and leaned against the cushions by the wall and drew her hand away from Khaled.

“What is it?” she asked in a low voice. “What is it you would have?” But though she had already asked the question many times she found no answer, and none that he was able to give could enlighten her darkness.

===Chinese===
“你不爱我，怎能懂我？”哈立德问，“爱有它自己的语言，两人相爱时，彼此都听得懂对方的话。可一个爱、一个不爱，两人便是陌生人，纵然是夫妻；又像波斯人（Persians）和阿拉伯人，谁也不懂谁的话；妻子喊‘父亲’，丈夫只当她渴了，端来一杯水。因为要说同一种语言，必得同心；要同心，必得彼此相爱。”

泽霍瓦叹了口气，靠在墙边的软垫上，把手从哈立德手中抽了回来。

“到底是什么？”她低声问，“你到底要什么？”这问题她已问过许多遍，却问不出答案，而他所能给出的任何回答，也照不亮她的黑暗。

===Original===
“It is the spark that kindles the flame,” Khaled said, and he pointed to the lights that hung in the room. “Your beauty is like that of a cunningly designed lamp, inlaid with gold and silver and covered with rich ornament, which is seen by day. But there is no light within, and it is cold, though it be full of oil and the wick be ready.”

Zehowah turned towards him somewhat impatiently.

“And you are as one who would kindle the flame with words, having no torch,” she answered.

===Chinese===
“是点燃火焰的那粒火星，”哈立德说着，指向悬在室中的灯，“你的美就像一盏精工细作的灯，金银镶嵌，满饰华纹，白日里尽可观赏。可里面没有光，它是冷的，纵然盛满了油，灯芯也早已备好。”

泽霍瓦有些不耐烦地转向他。

“那你就像一个人，手里没有火把，只想用言语把火点着，”她答道。

===Original===
“Have I not done deeds also?” asked Khaled. “Or have I spoken much, that you should reproach me? Surely I have slain more of your enemies than I have spoken words to you tonight.”

“But have I asked for an offering of blood, or a marriage dower of dead bodies?”

Khaled was silent, for he was bitterly disappointed, and as his eyes fell upon the sword which hung on the wall, he felt that he could almost have taken it and made an end of Zehowah for very anger that she would not love him. Had he not gone out for her into the raging heat of summer, and borne the burden of a great war, and destroyed a nation and taken a city? Moreover, if neither words nor deeds could gain her love, what means remained to him to try?

All through the night Khaled pondered, calling up all that he had seen in the world in former times, until he fell asleep at last, wearied in heart.

===Chinese===
“我难道没有做过事迹吗？”哈立德问，“我难道话多，至于让你责备？今夜我对你说的话，实在还没有我杀你仇敌杀得多。”

“可我几时要过血的祭献，几时要过用尸首充数的聘礼？”

哈立德沉默了，他失望已极。目光落到墙上挂着的剑上时，他觉得自己怒极之下，几乎要取下剑来结果了泽霍瓦，只因她不肯爱他。他不是为她顶着盛夏酷暑出征，担起一场大战的重担，灭了一个国家、攻下一座城池吗？何况，言语和事迹都换不来她的爱，他还剩什么法子可试？

哈立德整夜思量，把旧时在世上见过的一切都想了起来，直到心力交瘁，终于睡去。
===Original===
Very early in the morning one of Zehowah’s women came and stood by his bed and waked him. He could see that her face was pale in the dawn, her limbs trembled and her voice was uncertain.

“Arise, my lord!” she said. “A messenger has come from the army with evil news, and stands waiting in the court.”

Khaled sprang up, and Zehowah awoke also.

“What is this message?” he asked hastily.

===Chinese===
清晨极早，泽霍瓦的一名侍女走来立在床边唤醒他。他看得出她在晨光里脸色发白，四肢颤抖，声音不定。

“起来，我的主人！”她说，“军中来了信使，带来凶信，正在庭院里候着。”

哈立德一跃而起，泽霍瓦也醒了。

“是什么信？”他急忙问。

===Original===
But the woman threw herself upon the floor and covered her face, as though begging forgiveness because she brought evil tidings.

“Speak!” said Zehowah. “What is it?”

“Our lord the Sultan is dead!” cried the woman, and she broke out into weeping and crying and would say nothing more.

But when Zehowah heard that her father was dead, she sat down upon the floor and beat her breast and tore her hair, and wailed and wept, while all the women of the harem came and gathered round her and joined in her mourning, so that the whole palace was filled with the noise of their lamentations.

===Chinese===
那侍女却扑倒在地，掩住脸，仿佛因带来凶信而乞恕。

“说！”泽霍瓦道，“什么事？”

“我们的主上苏丹驾崩了！”侍女哭喊道，随即放声痛哭，再不肯多说一句。

泽霍瓦听得父亲死了，便坐倒在地，捶胸扯发，号哭起来。内院的女人们都跑来围着她一同举哀，整座宫里充满了哀哭之声。

===Original===
Khaled went out into the court and questioned the messenger, who told him that the Sultan had held a great feast in the evening in the gardens of Dereyiyah, having with him the woman Almasta and the other captive women, and being served by black slaves. But, suddenly, in the night, when most of the soldiers were already asleep, there had been a great cry, and the slaves and women had come running from the tent, crying that the Sultan was dead. This was true, and the Jewish physician who had gone out with his master declared that he had died from an access of humours to the head, brought on by a surfeit of sweetmeats, there being at the time an evil conjunction of Zoharah and Al Marech in square aspect to the moon and in the house of death.

Khaled therefore mounted his bay mare and rode quickly out to Dereyiyah, where he found that the news was true, and the women were already preparing the Sultan’s body for burial. Having ordered the mourning, and commanded the army to prepare for the return to the city, Khaled set out with the funeral procession; and when he reached the walls of Riad he turned to the left and passed round to the northeast side of the city where the burial-ground is situated. Here he laid the body of his father-in-law in the tomb which the latter had prepared for himself during his lifetime, and afterwards, dismissing the mourners, he went back into the city to the palace.

===Chinese===
哈立德走到庭中盘问信使。信使说，苏丹晚间在德拉伊耶的园子里大设盛宴，阿尔玛斯塔和其余被俘女子都在身边，又有黑奴侍奉。到了夜里，大多数士兵已经睡熟，忽然一声大喊，奴隶和女人们从帐中奔出，哭喊苏丹死了。这是真的。随主人出行的犹太医生宣布，苏丹死于体液上冲于头，起因是甜食过量；其时适逢金星佐哈拉（Zoharah）与火星马雷奇（Al Marech）凶会，与月亮成四分相，且入死宫。

哈立德于是跨上他的枣红母马，疾驰赶往德拉伊耶，发现凶信属实，女人们已在为苏丹的遗体做下葬的准备。哈立德安排了丧仪，命大军准备回城，随后随送葬的队伍出发；抵达里雅得城墙下时，他向左转，绕到城东北面的墓地。他把岳父的遗体安放进老人生前为自己备好的墓穴，然后遣散送葬的人，回城入宫。

===Original===
After the days of mourning were accomplished, the will of the Sultan was made known, though indeed the people were well acquainted with it already. By his will Khaled succeeded to the sovereignty of the kingdom of Nejed and to all the riches and treasures which the Sultan had accumulated during his lifetime. But the people received the announcement with acclamations and much joy, followed by a great feasting, for which innumerable camels were slain. Khaled also called all the chief officers and courtiers to a banquet and addressed them in a few words, according to his manner.

“Men of Nejed,” he said, “it has pleased Allah to remove to the companionship of the faithful our master the Sultan, my revered father-in-law, upon whom be peace, and to set me up among you as King in his stead, being the husband of his only daughter, which you all know. As for the past, you know me; but if I have wronged any man let him declare it and I will make reparation. And if not, let none complain hereafter. But as for the future I will be a just ruler so long as I live, and will lead the men of Nejed to war, when there is war, and will divide the spoil fairly; and in peace I will not oppress the people with taxes nor change the just and good laws of the kingdom. And now the feast is prepared. Sit down cheerfully, and may Allah give us both the appetite to enjoy and the strength to digest all the good things which shall be set before us.”

===Chinese===
服丧期满，苏丹的遗嘱公布了，其实百姓早已尽知。依着遗嘱，哈立德继承了内志（Nejed）王国的王位，以及苏丹毕生积聚的全部财富珍宝。百姓闻讯欢声雷动，喜气洋洋，随后大摆筵席，宰了无数骆驼。哈立德又召众将领与朝臣赴宴，照他的习惯，简短地讲了几句话。

“内志的众卿家，”他说，“安拉乐意把我们的主上苏丹——我敬爱的岳父，愿他安息——召往信士们的伴侣中去，又立我在你们中间代他为王，因为我是他独生女之夫，这你们尽人皆知。过去的事，你们了解我；我若亏待过谁，让他直说，我必补偿。若没有，往后便谁也不要抱怨。至于将来，只要我一息尚存，必为明主；有战事时，我率领内志的儿郎出征，战利品公平分派；太平年月，我不以赋税苦民，不改王国良法善制。如今筵席已备，诸位开怀入座吧，愿安拉赐我们胃口享用、气力消化摆在面前的种种美馔。”

===Original===
But Khaled himself ate sparingly, for his heart was heavy, and when they had feasted and drunk *treng* juice and heard music, he retired to the harem, where he found Zehowah sitting with Almasta, the Georgian woman, there being no other women present in the room. He was surprised when he saw Almasta, though he knew that the captive women had been lodged in the palace, the distribution of the spoil from the war having been put off by the mourning for the Sultan.

When Almasta heard him enter, she looked up quickly and a bright colour rose in her face, as when the juice of a pomegranate is poured into milk, and disappeared again as the false dawn before morning, leaving no trace. Khaled sat down.

===Chinese===
哈立德自己却吃得很少，因为他心情沉重。宴罢饮过*特伦格*果汁（treng），听了音乐，他便退往内院，见泽霍瓦正和格鲁吉亚女子阿尔玛斯塔坐在一处，房中并无别的女人。见了阿尔玛斯塔他有些惊讶，虽然他知道被俘女子都被安置在宫里——因苏丹之丧，战利品的分派被耽搁了下来。

阿尔玛斯塔听得他进来，急急抬头，脸上泛起一片鲜亮的红晕，像石榴汁倾入奶中，又像黎明前的假晓一闪而逝，不留痕迹。哈立德坐下了。

===Original===
“Is not this the woman of whom you spoke?” Zehowah asked. “I knew her from the rest by her red hair.”

“This is the woman. Your father would have taken her for his wife. But Allah has disposed otherwise.”

“She is beautiful. She is worthy to be a king’s wife,” said Zehowah.

“The Sultan?” asked Almasta, for she hardly understood. Her face turned as white as bone bleached by the sun, and her fingers trembled, while her eyes were cast down.

===Chinese===
“这不就是你说的那个女子吗？”泽霍瓦问，“我一眼就从那头红发里认出她来了。”

“正是她。你父亲本要娶她为妻，可安拉另有安排。”

“她很美，配得上做王的妻子，”泽霍瓦说。

“苏丹？”阿尔玛斯塔问，因为她不大听得懂。她的脸白得像太阳晒白的骨头，手指发颤，双眼低垂。

===Original===
Zehowah looked at Khaled and laughed.

“See how she trembles and turns pale before you,” she said. “And a little while ago her face was red. You have found a torch wherewith to kindle this lamp, and a breath that can extinguish it.”

“I do not know,” Khaled answered. But he looked attentively at Almasta and remained silent for some time. “It is now necessary to divide the spoils of the war,” he said at last, “and to bestow such of these women as you do not wish to keep upon the most deserving of the officers.”

===Chinese===
泽霍瓦看看哈立德，笑了。

“瞧她在你面前抖成这样，脸都白了，”她说，“方才她脸还是红的呢。你已找到了点燃这盏灯的火把，也找到了能把它吹熄的一口气。”

“我不知道，”哈立德答道。但他凝神看着阿尔玛斯塔，沉默了好一阵。“如今该分派战利品了，”他终于说，“这些女子，你不愿留的，就赏给最有功的将官。”

===Original===
“My lord will surely take the fairest for himself, since she loves him,” said Zehowah, again laughing, but somewhat bitterly.

“May my tongue be cloven and my eyes be put out, may my hands wither at the wrists and my feet fall from my ankles, if I ever take any wife but you,” said Khaled. “*Yallah!* So be it.”

When Zehowah heard him say this, even while Almasta’s face was unveiled before him, she understood that he was greatly in earnest.

===Chinese===
“她既然爱他，我的主人想必要把最美的留给自己了，”泽霍瓦说，又笑起来，笑里却有些苦涩。

“我若另娶一妻、不娶你，就让我的舌头裂开、双眼被剜，双手从腕上枯落、双脚从踝上断掉，”哈立德说，“*雅拉*！（Yallah！）就这样吧。”

泽霍瓦听他这样说——当着阿尔玛斯塔揭了面纱的脸——明白他是万分认真的。

===Original===
“Let me keep her for my handmaid,” she said at last.

“Is she mine that you need ask me? But it will be wiser to give her to Abdul Kerim, the sheikh of the horsemen. I have promised that the spoil should be fairly divided, and though few have seen this woman many have heard of her beauty. And besides, she would weary you, for she cannot talk in Arabian, nor does she seem quick to learn. Abdul Kerim has the first right, since Allah has removed your father, upon whom be peace.”

“Your words are my laws,” answered Zehowah obediently. “And, indeed, it may be that you are right, for I believe she can neither dance nor sing, nor play upon any musical instrument. She would certainly weary me after a time, as you say. Give her therefore to Abdul Kerim for his share.”

===Chinese===
“那就把她留给我做侍女吧，”她终于说。

“难道她是我的，还用得着你问我？不过把她给了阿卜杜勒·克里姆（Abdul Kerim），骑兵的首领，更为妥当。我许诺过战利品要公平分派；见过这女子的虽少，听过她美貌的却多。再说，她会让你厌烦的，她不会讲阿拉伯话，学得也不见得快。安拉既召走了你父亲——愿他安息——阿卜杜勒·克里姆理当居首。”

“你的话就是我的法度，”泽霍瓦顺从地答道，“说真的，也许你是对的，我想她既不会跳舞，也不会唱歌，什么乐器都不会弄。照你说的，过些时候她准会让我腻烦。那就把她分给阿卜杜勒·克里姆，算他的一份。”

===Original===
They then made Almasta understand that she was to be given to the sheikh of the horsemen; but when she had understood she shook her head and smiled, though at first she said nothing, so that Khaled and Zehowah wondered whether she had comprehended what they had told her.

“Do you understand what we have told you?” asked Zehowah, who was diverted by her ignorance of the Arabic language.

“I understand.”

“And are you not pleased that you are to be the wife of Abdul Kerim, who is a rich man and still young?”

===Chinese===
随后他们让阿尔玛斯塔明白，她将被赐给骑兵首领；可她弄明白之后，只是摇头微笑，起初一言不发，倒叫哈立德和泽霍瓦疑惑她到底听懂了没有。

“你听得懂我们说的话吗？”泽霍瓦问，她被她不通阿拉伯语的样子逗乐了。

“听得懂。”

“你要做阿卜杜勒·克里姆的妻子了，他有钱，人也还年轻，你不高兴吗？”

===Original===
“I was to be the Sultan’s wife,” said Almasta, with difficulty, looking at Khaled. “You told me so.”

“The Sultan is dead,” Khaled answered.

“Who is the Sultan now?” she asked.

“Khaled is the Sultan,” said Zehowah.

===Chinese===
“我本来是要做苏丹的妻子的，”阿尔玛斯塔吃力地说，眼睛望着哈立德，“是你对我说的。”

“苏丹死了，”哈立德答道。

“如今谁是苏丹？”她问。

“哈立德是苏丹，”泽霍瓦说。

===Original===
“You said that I should be the Sultan’s wife,” Almasta repeated.

“Doubtless, I said so,” Khaled replied. “But Allah has ordered it otherwise.”

Almasta again smiled and shook her head.

===Chinese===
“你说过我要做苏丹的妻子的，”阿尔玛斯塔重复道。

“不错，我说过，”哈立德答道，“可安拉的安排不是这样。”

阿尔玛斯塔又笑了，摇了摇头。

## VI / 六

===Original===
On the following day Khaled made a division of the spoils, and gave Almasta to Abdul Kerim, enjoining upon him to marry her, since he had but two wives and could do so lawfully. The sheikh of the horsemen was glad, for he had heard much of Almasta’s beauty, and he loved fair women, being of a fierce temper and not more than forty years old. So he called his friends to the marriage feast that same day, and Zehowah sent Almasta in a litter to his harem, giving her also numerous rich garments by way of a dower, but which in fact were due to Abdul Kerim as his share of the booty. So the men feasted, with music, until the evening, when the bridegroom retired to the harem and the Kadi came and read the contract; after which Abdul Kerim sat down while Almasta was brought before him in various dresses, one after the other, as is customary.

When the women were all gone away, Abdul Kerim began to talk to his wife, but she only laughed and said the few words she knew, not knowing what he said, and presently she began to sing to him in a low voice, in her own language. Her voice was very clear and quite different from that of the Arabian women whom Abdul had heard, and the tones vibrated with great passion and sweetness, so that he was enchanted and listened, as in a dream, while his head rested against Almasta’s knee. She continued to sing in such a manner that his soul was transported with delight; and at last, as the sound soothed him, he fell into a gentle sleep.

Almasta, still singing softly, loosened his vest, touching him so gently that he did not wake. She then drew out of one of the three tresses of her hair a fine steel needle, extremely long and sharp, having at one end a small wooden ball for a handle, and while she sang, she thrust it very quickly into his breast to its full length, so that it pierced his heart and he died instantly. But she continued to sing, lest any of the women should be listening from a distance. Presently she withdrew the needle so slowly that not a drop of blood followed it, and having made it pass thrice through the carpet she restored it to her hair, after which she fastened the dead man’s vest again, so that nothing was disarranged. She sang on, after this for some time, and then after a short silence she sprang up from the couch, uttering loud screams and lamentations and beating her breast violently.

The women of the harem came in quickly, and when they saw that their master was dead, they sat down with Almasta and wept with her, for as he lay dead there was no mark of any violence nor any sign whereby it could be told that he had not died naturally.

===Chinese===
次日，哈立德分派了战利品，把阿尔玛斯塔（Almasta）给了阿卜杜勒·克里姆（Abdul Kerim），嘱他娶她为妻，因为他只有两房妻室，再娶一房也合乎教法。骑兵首领很欢喜，他早听闻阿尔玛斯塔的美貌，又素爱美人，性子暴烈，年纪还不满四十。他当天便请来亲友赴婚宴，泽霍瓦用轿辇把阿尔玛斯塔送到他的内院，还陪嫁了许多华贵衣裳——其实这些本就是阿卜杜勒·克里姆应得的战利份额。男子们伴着音乐欢宴到傍晚，新郎退入内院，卡迪（Kadi，教法官）前来诵读了婚书；随后阿卜杜勒·克里姆坐下，阿尔玛斯塔按习俗换着各色衣裳，一件一件地被带到他面前。

女人们都退去后，阿卜杜勒·克里姆开始同妻子说话，她却只是笑，说她会的那几个词，全不知他说的是什么，过了一会儿，她开始用自己的语言低声为他唱歌。她的嗓音清亮极了，与阿卜杜勒听过的阿拉伯女子全然不同，声调里颤动着浓烈的情意与甜美，他听得心醉神迷，像在梦中一样，头倚着阿尔玛斯塔的膝听下去。她唱得他灵魂出窍，欣喜若狂；最后，歌声抚慰着他，他沉沉睡去。

阿尔玛斯塔依旧轻轻唱着，解开他的背心，动作轻得没有惊醒他。随后她从自己的三股发辫之一里抽出一根细钢针，极长极锐，一端有个小木球作柄。她边唱边疾速将针整根刺进他的胸口，穿透心脏，他当即死了。她却继续唱着，惟恐有哪个女人在远处听见。过了一会儿，她把针缓缓抽出，慢得不带出一滴血，又让针在地毯上穿插三下，收回发间，再把死者的背心系好，什么也没有弄乱。此后她又唱了一阵，然后沉默片刻，猛地从榻上跳起来，放声尖叫哀号，狠狠捶打自己的胸膛。

内院的女人们急忙进来，见主人已死，便陪阿尔玛斯塔坐下，同她一起哭；因为他死在那里，身上没有半点暴力的痕迹，也没有任何迹象表明他不是自然死亡。

===Original===
When Khaled heard that Abdul Kerim was dead, he was much grieved at heart, for the man had been brave and had been often at his right hand in battle. But the news being brought to him at dawn when he awoke, he immediately sent the Jewish physician of the court to ascertain if possible the cause of the sudden death. The physician made careful examination of the body, and having purified himself returned to Khaled to give an account.

“I have executed my lord’s orders with scrupulous exactness,” he said, “and I find that without doubt the sheikh of the horsemen died suddenly by an access of humours to the heart, the sun being at that time in the nadir, for he died about midnight, and being moreover in evil conjunction with the Dragon’s Tail in the Heart of the Lion, and not yet far from the square aspect of Al Marech which caused the death of his majesty the late Sultan, upon whom be peace.”

But Khaled was thoughtful, for he reflected that this was the second time that a man had died suddenly when he was about to be Almasta’s husband, and he remembered, how she had attempted to kill the Sultan of Haïl, and had ultimately brought about his death.

“Have you examined the dead man as minutely as you have observed the stars?” he inquired. “Is there no mark of violence upon him, nor of poison, nor of strangling?”

===Chinese===
哈立德听说阿卜杜勒·克里姆死了，心里非常悲痛，因为此人英勇，在战场上常在他右手边作战。消息是他清晨醒来时传来的，他立即派宫里的犹太医生去查明暴毙的原因。医生仔细检验了尸体，行过洁净礼后，回来向哈立德禀报。

“我一丝不苟地奉行了主人的吩咐，”他说，“我查明，骑兵首领无疑死于体液上冲于心；其时太阳正在天底，因为他约在半夜死去；况且又与狮心处的龙尾凶会，而且尚未远离那令先苏丹陛下——愿他安息——殒命的马雷奇（Al Marech）四分相。”

哈立德却沉吟起来，他想到这是第二个将要娶阿尔玛斯塔为妻而暴毙的男人，又记起她如何行刺哈伊勒（Haïl）的苏丹未遂，最终还是要了他的命。

“你验这死人，有你观星那样细吗？”他问道，“他身上没有暴力的痕迹？没有中毒的？没有勒扼的？”

===Original===
“There is no mark. By Allah! I speak truth. My lord may see for himself, for the man is not yet buried.”

“Am I a jackal, that I should sniff at dead bodies?” asked Khaled. “Go in peace.”

The physician withdrew, for he saw that Khaled was displeased, and he was himself as much surprised as anyone by the death of Abdul Kerim, a man lean and strong, not given to surfeiting and in the prime of health.

“*Min Allah!*” he said as he departed. “We are in the hand of the Lord, who knoweth our rising up and our lying down. It is possible that if I had seen this man at the moment of death, or a little before, I might have discovered the nature of his disease, for I could have talked with him and questioned him.”

===Chinese===
“没有痕迹。以安拉（Allah）起誓！我说的是实话。主人可以亲自去看，人还没有下葬。”

“难道我是豺狼，要去嗅尸体吗？”哈立德问，“去吧，愿你平安。”

医生退下了，他看出哈立德不悦。阿卜杜勒·克里姆精瘦强健，饮食有节，正当盛年，他的死连医生自己也和别人一样惊讶。

“*安拉在上*！（Min Allah!）”他临走时说，“我们在主的手中，主晓得我们的起与卧。倘若我在这人断气之时、或稍早一点见到他，或许能查出他的病症，因为我本可以同他说话，问他话。”

===Original===
But Khaled went in and talked with Zehowah. She was greatly astonished when she heard that Almasta’s husband was dead, but she was satisfied with the answer of the Jewish physician, who enjoyed great reputation and was believed to be at that time the wisest man in Arabia.

“Give her back to me, to be one of my women,” said she. “It is not written that she should marry a man of Nejed, unless you will take her yourself.”

But Khaled bent his brow angrily and his eyes glowed like the coals of a camp fire which is almost extinguished, when the night wind blows suddenly over the ashes.

“I have spoken,” he said.

===Chinese===
哈立德进宫同泽霍瓦说起了这事。听说阿尔玛斯塔的丈夫死了，她大为惊讶，但犹太医生的答复让她信服——那医生名望极高，时人公认是阿拉伯（Arabia）第一智者。

“把她还给我，做我的侍女吧，”她说，“命书上没写她该嫁内志（Nejed）的男人，除非你自己娶她。”

哈立德怒容满面，双眼像将熄的营火余烬，夜风骤然吹过灰堆时又亮了起来。

“我已经说过了，”他说。

===Original===
“And I have heard,” she answered. “Let there be an end. But give me this woman to divert me with her broken speech.”

“I fear she will do you an injury of which you may not live,” said Khaled.

“What injury can she do me?” asked Zehowah in astonishment, not understanding him.

“She asked of your father the head of the Sultan of Haïl, whom she hated. And your father gave it to her.”

===Chinese===
“我也听见了，”她答道，“这事就到这里。可把这女人给我吧，让她那半生不熟的话给我解闷。”

“我怕她害你性命，”哈立德说。

“她能害我什么？”泽霍瓦惊讶地问，不明白他的意思。

“她向你父亲讨过哈伊勒苏丹的头颅，就是她恨的那个人。你父亲给了她。”

===Original===
“Peace be upon him!” exclaimed Zehowah piously.

“Upon him peace. And when he would have married her, he died suddenly at the feasting. And now this Abdul Kerim, who was to have been her husband, is dead also, without sign, in the night, as a man stung by a serpent in his sleep. These are strange doings.”

“If you think she has done evil, let her be put to death,” said Zehowah. “But the physician found no mark upon Abdul Kerim. By the hand of Allah he was taken.”

“Doubtless his fate was about his neck. But it is strange.”

===Chinese===
“愿他平安！”泽霍瓦虔敬地呼道。

“愿他平安。他正要娶她，就在席上暴毙了。如今这将要娶她为妻的阿卜杜勒·克里姆，也毫无征兆地死在夜里，像睡梦中被蛇咬死的人一样。这些事蹊跷。”

“你若以为她作了恶，就把她处死，”泽霍瓦说，“可医生在阿卜杜勒·克里姆身上没找到痕迹。他是被安拉亲手收去的。”

“他的劫数无疑早已悬在他颈上了。可这事蹊跷。”

===Original===
Zehowah looked at Khaled in silence, but presently she smiled and laid her hand upon his.

“This woman loves you with her whole soul,” she said. “You think that she has slain Abdul Kerim by secret arts, in the hope that she may marry you.”

“And your father also.”

Then they were both silent, and Zehowah covered her face, since she could not prevent tears from falling when she thought of her father, whom she had loved.

===Chinese===
泽霍瓦默默地望着哈立德，随即又笑了，把手放在他的手上。

“这女人是全心全意爱你，”她说，“你以为她用邪术杀了阿卜杜勒·克里姆，指望能嫁给你。”

“还有你父亲。”

两人都沉默了。泽霍瓦掩住脸，因为一想到深爱的父亲，她忍不住落泪。

===Original===
“If this be so,” she said after a long time, “let the woman die immediately.”

“It is necessary to be just,” Khaled answered. “I will put no one to death without witnesses, not even a captive woman, who is certainly an unbeliever at heart. Has anyone seen her do these deeds, or does anyone know by what means a man may be slain in his sleep, or at a feast, so that no mark is left upon his body? At Dereyiyah your father was alone with her in the inner part of the tent, and she was singing to him that he might sleep. For I have made inquiry. And when Abdul Kerim died he was also alone with her. I cannot understand these things. But you are a woman and subtle. It may be that you can see what is too dark for me.”

“It may be. Therefore give her back to me, and I will lay a trap for her, so that she will betray herself if she has really done evil. And when we have convicted her by her own words she shall die.”

“Are you not afraid, Zehowah?”

“Can I change my destiny? If my hour is come, I shall die of a fever, or of a cold, whether she be with me or not. But if my years are not full, she cannot hurt me.”

“This is undoubtedly true,” answered Khaled, who could find nothing to say. “But I will first question the woman myself.”

===Chinese===
“若真如此，”她过了许久才说，“就让这女人立刻死。”

“行事必须公正，”哈立德答道，“没有证人，我不处死任何人，哪怕是个被俘的女人——她心里无疑是个不信教的人。有谁见过她做这些事？有谁知道用什么法子，能让一个人在睡梦中、在宴席上被杀，身上却不留痕迹？在德拉伊耶（Dereyiyah），你父亲在帐内深处与她独处，她正唱歌哄他入睡——这我都查问过了。阿卜杜勒·克里姆死时，也是与她独处。这些事我参不透。但你是女人，心思细。或许我看着漆黑一片的地方，你能看得出。”

“也许吧。所以把她交还给我，我来给她设个套，她若真作了恶，自会露出马脚。等她自己的话定了她的罪，她就得死。”

“你不怕吗，泽霍瓦？”

“我的命数能改吗？我的时辰若到了，不管她在不在我身边，我发一场热、受一场寒都会死。我的寿数若未满，她伤不了我。”

“这无疑是真的，”哈立德答道，他也找不出别的话说，“不过我要先亲自审问那女人。”

===Original===
So he sent slaves with a litter to bring Almasta from the house of mourning to the palace, and when she was come he sent out all the other women and remained alone with her and Zehowah, making her sit down before him so that he could see her face. Her cheeks were pale, for she had not slept, having been occupied in weeping and lamentation during the whole night, and her eyes moved restlessly as those of a person distracted with grief.

Khaled then drew his sword and laid it across his feet as he sat and looked fixedly at Almasta.

“If you do not speak the truth,” he said, “I will cut off your head with my own hand. Allah is witness.”

===Chinese===
于是他派奴隶用轿辇把阿尔玛斯塔从服丧的宅子接进宫来。她到了以后，他打发走其余所有女人，只同她和泽霍瓦留下，让她坐在自己面前，好看清她的脸。她双颊苍白，因为整夜都在哭泣哀号，不曾合眼；两眼不安地转动，像被悲伤折磨得失了魂的人。

哈立德随即拔出剑来，横放在脚上，坐着定睛注视阿尔玛斯塔。

“你若不说实话，”他说，“我就亲手砍下你的头。安拉作证。”

===Original===
When Almasta saw the drawn sword, her face grew whiter than before, and for some moments she seemed not able to breathe. But suddenly she began to beat her breast, and broke out into loud wailings, rocking herself to and fro as she sat on the carpet.

“My husband is dead!” she cried. “He was young; he was beautiful! He is dead! *Wah! Wah!* my husband is dead! Kill me too!”

Khaled looked at Zehowah, but she said nothing, though she watched Almasta attentively. Then Khaled spoke to the woman again.

===Chinese===
阿尔玛斯塔见了出鞘的剑，脸比先前更白了，好一阵子像透不过气来。可她忽然捶起胸膛，放声哀号起来，坐在地毯上前后摇晃着身子。

“我的丈夫死了！”她哭喊道，“他年轻，他俊美！他死了！*哇！哇！*（Wah! Wah!）我的丈夫死了！把我也杀了吧！”

哈立德看看泽霍瓦，她一言不发，只是留神盯着阿尔玛斯塔。哈立德便又对那女人说话。
===Original===
“Make an end of lamenting for the present,” he said. “It has pleased Allah to take your husband to the fellowship of the faithful. Peace be upon him. Tell us in what manner he died, and what words he spoke when he felt his end approaching, for he was my good friend and I wish to know all.”

Almasta either did not understand or made a pretence of not understanding, but when she heard Khaled’s words she ceased from wailing and sobbed silently, beating her breast from time to time.

“How did he die?” Khaled asked in a stern voice.

“He was asleep. He died,” replied Almasta in broken tones.

===Chinese===
“暂且收了哭声吧，”他说，“安拉乐意把你的丈夫接到信士们的行列里去了。愿他平安。告诉我们他是怎样死的，他感到大限来临时说了什么话，因为他是我的好友，我要知道这一切。”

阿尔玛斯塔要么没听懂，要么装作没听懂，但听了哈立德的话，她止住了哀号，无声地抽泣，不时捶一捶胸。

“他是怎么死的？”哈立德声色俱厉地问。

“他睡着了。他死了，”阿尔玛斯塔断断续续地答道。

===Original===
“You will get no other answer,” said Zehowah. “She cannot speak our tongue.”

“Is there no woman among them all who can talk this woman’s language?” asked Khaled with impatience, for he saw how useless it was to question her.

“There is no one. I have inquired. Leave her with me, and if there is anything to be known, I will try to find it out.”

So Khaled went away and Zehowah endeavoured to soothe Almasta and make her talk in her broken words. But the woman made as though she would not be comforted, and went and sat apart upon the stone floor where there was no carpet, rocking to and fro, and wailing in a low voice. Zehowah understood that whatever the truth might be Almasta was determined to express her sorrow in the customary way, and that it would be better to leave her alone.

===Chinese===
“你得不到别的回答了，”泽霍瓦说，“她不会说我们的话。”

“她们当中就没有一个女人会说这女人的话吗？”哈立德不耐烦地问，因为他看出盘问她毫无用处。

“没有。我问过了。把她留给我，只要有可查的，我一定想法子查出来。”

哈立德走了，泽霍瓦设法安抚阿尔玛斯塔，想让她用那半生不熟的话开口。可这女人一副不肯受慰的样子，独自走到没铺地毯的石地上坐下，前后摇晃着，低声哀哭。泽霍瓦明白了：无论真相如何，阿尔玛斯塔是决意要按习俗把悲伤哭足的，不如由她一个人待着。

===Original===
For seven days she sat thus apart, covering her head and mourning, and refusing to speak with anyone, so that all the women supposed her to be indeed distracted with grief at the death of Abdul Kerim. And each day Khaled inquired of his wife whether she had yet learned anything, and received the same answer. But in the meantime he was occupied with his own thoughts, as well as with the affairs of the kingdom, though the latter were as nothing in his mind compared with the workings of his heart when he thought of Zehowah.

It chanced one evening that Khaled was riding among the gardens without the city, attended only by a few horsemen, for he was simple in all his ways and liked little to have a great throng of attendants about him. So he rode alone, while the horsemen followed at a distance.

“Was ever a man, or an angel, so placed in the world as I am placed?” he thought. “How much better would it have been had I never seen Zehowah, and if I had never slain the Indian prince. For I should still have been with my fellows, the genii, from whom I am now cut off, and at least I should have lived until the day of the resurrection. But now my horse may stumble and fall, and my neck may be broken, and there is no hereafter. Or I may die in my sleep, or be killed in my sleep, and there will be no resurrection for me, nor any more life, anywhere in Earth or heaven. For Zehowah will never love me. Was ever a man so placed? And I am ashamed to complain to her any more, for she is a good wife, obedient and careful of my wants, and beautiful as the moon at the full, rising amidst palm trees, besides being very wise and subtle. How can I complain? Has she not given me herself, whom I desired, and a great kingdom which, indeed, I did not desire, but which no man can despise as a gift? Yet I am burned up within, and my heart is melting as a piece of frankincense laid upon coals in an empty chamber, when no man cares for its sweet savour. Surely, I am the most wretched of mankind. Oh, that the angel who made garments for me of a *ghada* bush, and a bay mare of a locust, would come down and lay his hand upon Zehowah’s breast and make a living heart of the stone which Allah has set in its place!”

===Chinese===
她就这样独自坐了七天，蒙着头服丧，不肯同任何人说话，以致女人们都当真以为她为阿卜杜勒·克里姆之死悲恸欲狂。哈立德每天都问妻子可打听到什么，得到的回答都一样。其间他心里想自己的心事，也处理王国政务，但一想到泽霍瓦，心中的翻腾便让政务显得不值一提。

一天傍晚，哈立德恰好在城外的园囿间骑马，身边只有几名骑兵，因为他行事素来简朴，不喜欢一大群随从围着。他独自骑在前面，骑兵远远跟着。

“世上可曾有过一个人，或一个天使，落到我这般境地吗？”他想，“我若从未见过泽霍瓦，从未杀了那印度王子，该有多好。那样我如今仍同我的同类精灵（genii）们在一起，不至于像现在这样被隔绝；至少我还能活到死人复活之日。可现在，我的马一个失蹄摔倒，我的脖颈就可能折断，而死后什么也没有。我也可能在睡梦中死去，或在睡梦中被杀，我不会有复活，天上地下任何地方，都不会再有生命。因为泽霍瓦永远不会爱我。世上可曾有人落到这般境地？我再也没有脸向她抱怨了，因为她是位好妻子，温顺听话，体贴我的需要，美得像椰枣树间初升的满月，又极聪明、极细密。我还能抱怨什么？她不是把她自己给了我吗——那正是我所渴望的；不是还给了我一副大王国吗——我本不稀罕它，但作为馈赠，没有人会嫌弃。可我五内如焚，我的心像一块乳香，搁在空屋的炭火上慢慢熔尽，没人在意它的香气。诚然，我是世人中最不幸的。唉，那曾用*加达*（ghada）灌木为我变出衣裳、用一只蝗虫变出枣红母马的天使啊，但愿他降临，把手按在泽霍瓦的胸上，把安拉安放在那里的石头变作一颗活的心！”

===Original===
So he rode slowly on, reasoning as he had often reasoned before, and reaching the same conclusion in all his argument, which availed him nothing. But suddenly, as the sun went down, a new thought entered his mind and gave him a little hope.

“The sun is gone down,” he said to himself. “But Allah has not destroyed the sun. It will rise in the east tomorrow when the white cock crows in the first heaven. Many things have being, which the sight of man cannot see. It may be that although I see no signs of love in the heaven of Zehowah’s eyes, yet love is already there and will before long rise as the sun and illuminate my darkness. For I am not subtle as the evil genii are, but I must see very clearly before I am able to distinguish.”

He rode back into the city, planning how he might surprise Zehowah and obtain from her unawares some proof that she indeed loved him. To this end he entered the palace by a secret gate, covering his garments with his aba, and his head with the kefiyeh he wore, in order to disguise himself from the slaves and the soldiers whom he met on his way to the harem. He passed on towards Zehowah’s apartment by an unlighted passage not generally used, and hid himself in a niche of the wall close to the open door, from which he could see all that happened, and hear what was said.

===Chinese===
他缓缓骑行，像从前无数次那样思前想后，翻来覆去总得出同一个结论，于事无补。可就在太阳落山时，一个新念头忽然涌上心头，给了他一点希望。

“太阳落下去了，”他自语道，“可安拉并没有毁灭太阳。明天第一重天上的白鸡啼鸣时，它还会从东方升起。世上许多东西确然存在，人的眼睛却看不见。或许，泽霍瓦眼睛的天穹里虽看不见爱的迹象，爱却已在那里，不久便会像太阳一样升起，照亮我的黑暗。因为我不像邪恶的精灵那样心细，非得看得清清楚楚，才能分辨。”

他回城路上盘算着，如何出其不意地试探泽霍瓦，趁她不备拿到她确实爱他的凭证。为达此目的，他从一道秘门进了宫，用阿巴斗篷（aba）罩住衣裳，头上裹着他惯用的凯菲耶头巾（kefiyeh），免得在去内院的路上被奴隶和卫兵认出。他沿一条不常走人的暗道走向泽霍瓦的居室，藏在敞开的门旁墙壁凹龛里，从那里能看清里面发生的一切，听见所说的话。

===Original===
Zehowah was seated in her accustomed place and Almasta was beside her. Khaled could watch their faces by the light of the hanging lamps, as the two women talked together.

“You must put aside all mourning now,” Zehowah was saying. “For I will find another husband for you.”

“Another husband?” Almasta smiled and shook her head.

===Chinese===
泽霍瓦坐在她惯常的位子上，阿尔玛斯塔在她身旁。哈立德借着悬灯的光，看着两个女人说话时的脸。

“如今你必须收起丧服了，”泽霍瓦正说着，“因为我要给你再找一个丈夫。”

“再找丈夫？”阿尔玛斯塔笑着摇头。

===Original===
“Yes, there are other goodly men in Riad, though Abdul Kerim was of the goodliest, as all say who knew him. He was the Sultan’s friend, but he was more soldier than courtier. He deserved a better death.”

“Abdul Kerim died in peace. He was asleep.” Almasta smiled still, but more sadly, and her eyes were cast down.

“He died in peace,” Zehowah repeated, watching her narrowly. “But it is better to die in battle by the enemy’s hand. Such a man, falling in the front of the fight for the true faith, enters immediately into paradise, to dwell forever under the perpetual shade of the tree Sedrat, and neither blackness nor shame shall cover his face. There the rivers flow with milk and with clarified honey, and he shall rest on a couch covered with thick silk embroidered with gold, and shall possess seventy beautiful virgins whose eyes are blacker than mine and their skin whiter than yours, having colour like rubies and pearls, and their voices like the song of nightingales in Ajjem, of which travellers tell. These are the rewards of the true believer as set forth in Al Koran by our prophet, upon whom peace. A man slain in battle for the faith enters directly into the possession of all this, but unbelievers shall be taken by the forelock and the heels and cast into hell, to drink boiling molten brass, as a thirsty camel drinks clear water.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，里雅得（Riad）还有别的俊美男子，虽然认识阿卜杜勒·克里姆的人都说他是其中的佼佼者。他是苏丹的朋友，但他与其说是朝臣，不如说是战士。他不该死得那样。”

“阿卜杜勒·克里姆走得平安。他睡着了。”阿尔玛斯塔仍笑着，却笑得更哀伤，双眼低垂。

“他走得平安，”泽霍瓦重复道，眼睛紧盯着她，“可死在敌人的刀下、殁于战阵才是更好的死法。这样的人为正教倒在战阵最前，随即进入天园，永居赛德拉特树（Sedrat）不落的浓荫之下，黑影与耻辱都不会蒙上他的脸。那里河流淌着奶与净蜜，他安卧在金绣厚锦的榻上，拥有七十位美丽的处女，她们的眼睛比我的黑，皮肤比你的白，色如红宝石与珍珠，声音像旅人们传说的阿杰姆（Ajjem）夜莺的歌唱。这些便是《古兰经》（Al Koran）中我们的先知——愿他平安——所昭示的信士的报偿。为信仰战死的人径直得享这一切；而不信的人，将被揪住额发和脚跟扔进地狱，饮滚沸的铜汁，像干渴的骆驼畅饮清水。”

===Original===
Almasta understood very little of what Zehowah said, but she smiled, nevertheless, catching the meaning of some of the words.

“The Sultan Khaled loves black eyes,” she said. “He will go to paradise.”

“Doubtless, he will quench his thirst in the incorruptible milk of heavenly rivers,” Zehowah replied. “He is the chief of the brave, the light of the faith and the burning torch of righteousness. Otherwise Allah would not have chosen him to rule. But I spoke of Abdul Kerim.”

“He died in peace,” said Almasta the second time, and again looking down.

===Chinese===
泽霍瓦说的这些，阿尔玛斯塔懂得极少，不过还是抓住了几个词的意思，微笑着。

“哈立德苏丹爱黑眼睛，”她说，“他会进天园的。”

“无疑，他会在天国河流那不朽的奶汁里解渴，”泽霍瓦答道，“他是勇者之首，正教之光，正义的火炬，不然安拉也不会拣选他为王。可我说的是阿卜杜勒·克里姆。”

“他走得平安，”阿尔玛斯塔第二次这样说，又垂下了眼。

===Original===
“I do not know how he died,” Zehowah answered, looking steadily at the woman’s face. “It was a great misfortune for you. Do you understand? I am very sorry for you. You would have been happy with Abdul Kerim.”

“I mourn for him,” Almasta said, not raising her eyes.

“It is natural and right. Doubtless you loved him as soon as you saw him.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道他是怎么死的，”泽霍瓦答道，目光定定地看着这女人的脸，“这对你是大不幸。你懂吗？我很为你难过。你同阿卜杜勒·克里姆本会过得很幸福。”

“我为他服丧，”阿尔玛斯塔说着，没有抬眼。

“这是人情，也是本分。不用说，你一见到他就爱上他了。”

===Original===
Almasta glanced quickly at Zehowah, as though suspecting a hidden meaning in the words, and for a moment each of the women looked into the other’s eyes, but Zehowah saw nothing. For a wise man has truly said that one may see into the depths of black eyes as into a deep well, but that blue eyes are like the sea of Oman in winter, sparkling in the sun as a plain of blue sand, but underneath more unfathomable than the desert.

Almasta was too wise and deceitful to let the silence last. So when she had looked at Zehowah and understood, she smiled somewhat sorrowfully and spoke.

“I could have loved him,” she said. “I desire no husband now.”

===Chinese===
阿尔玛斯塔飞快地瞥了泽霍瓦一眼，仿佛疑心这话里藏着话。两个女人四目相对了一瞬，可泽霍瓦什么也没看出来。因为智者说过：黑眼睛像深井，人可以一直望到底；蓝眼睛却像冬日的阿曼海（Oman），在太阳底下闪着光，像一片蓝色的沙原，底下却比沙漠更深不可测。

阿尔玛斯塔机敏狡黠，不会让沉默持续下去。她看了看泽霍瓦，明白了她的意思，便微露哀色地笑着开了口。

“我本可以爱他的，”她说，“如今我不想再要丈夫了。”

===Original===
“That is not true,” Zehowah answered quickly. “You wish to marry Khaled, and that is the reason why you killed Abdul Kerim.”

Almasta started as a camel struck by a flight of locusts.

“What is this lie?” she cried out with indignation. “Who has told you this lie?” But her face was as grey as a stone, and her lips trembled.

“You probably killed him by magic arts learned in your own country,” said Zehowah quietly. “Do not be afraid. We are alone, and no one can hear us. Tell me how you killed him. Truly it was very skilful of you, since the physician, who is the wisest man in Arabia, could not tell how it was done.”

===Chinese===
“这不是实话，”泽霍瓦立刻答道，“你想嫁给哈立德，所以你才杀了阿卜杜勒·克里姆。”

阿尔玛斯塔惊跳起来，像被蝗群扑面的骆驼。

“这是什么谎话？”她愤慨地叫道，“谁对你说的这个谎？”可她的脸灰得像石头，嘴唇直颤。

“你多半是用你本国学的邪术杀了他，”泽霍瓦平静地说，“别怕。这里只有我们俩，没人听得见。告诉我你是怎么杀的。说实话，你干得真巧妙，连阿拉伯第一智者的医生都看不出是怎么做的。”
===Original===
But Almasta began to beat her breast and to make oaths and asseverations in her own language, which Zehowah could not understand.

“If you will tell me how you did it, I will give you a rich gift,” Zehowah continued.

But so much the more Almasta cried out, stretching her hands upwards and speaking incomprehensible words. So Zehowah waited until she became quiet again.

“It may be that Khaled will marry you, if you will tell me your secret,” Zehowah said, after a time.

===Chinese===
阿尔玛斯塔却捶起胸来，用自己的语言赌咒发誓，泽霍瓦一句也听不懂。

“你若肯告诉我你是怎么做的，我送你一份厚礼，”泽霍瓦接着说。

阿尔玛斯塔却叫得更凶了，双手举向天上，说着谁也不懂的话。泽霍瓦便等着，等她重新安静下来。

“你若肯把你的秘密告诉我，哈立德说不定会娶你，”过了一会儿，泽霍瓦说。

===Original===
Then Almasta’s cheek burned and she bent down her eyes.

“Will you tell me how to kill a man and leave no trace?” asked Zehowah, still pressing her. “Look at this pearl. Is it not beautiful? See how well it looks upon your hair. It is as the leaf of a white rose upon a river of red gold. And on your neck﻿—you cannot see it yourself﻿—it is like the full moon hanging upon a milky cloud. Khaled would give you many pearls like this, if he married you. Will you not tell me?”

“Whom do you wish to kill?” Almasta asked, very suddenly. But Zehowah was unmoved.

===Chinese===
阿尔玛斯塔的脸颊顿时烧红了，她垂下眼睛。

“你肯告诉我，怎样才能杀了人又不留痕迹吗？”泽霍瓦仍紧追不舍，“看这颗珍珠。美不美？瞧它衬在你头发上多好看。像红色黄金的河面上浮着一片白玫瑰的花瓣。戴在你颈上——你自己看不见——就像满月悬在一朵乳白的云上。哈立德若娶了你，会给你许多这样的珍珠。你还不肯告诉我吗？”

“你想杀谁？”阿尔玛斯塔突然问道。泽霍瓦却不为所动。

===Original===
“It may be that I have a private enemy,” she said. “Perhaps there is one who disturbs me, against whom I plot in the night, but can find no way of ridding myself of him. A woman might give much to destroy such a one.”

“Khaled will kill your enemies. He loves you. He will kill all whom you hate.”

“You make progress. You speak our language better,” said Zehowah, laughing a little. “You will soon be able to tell the Sultan that you love him, as well as I could myself.”

===Chinese===
“也许我有个私仇，”她说，“或许有个人搅扰我，我夜里都在盘算他，却找不出除掉他的法子。为了毁掉这样一个人，一个女人什么代价都肯出。”

“哈立德会杀你的仇敌。他爱你。你恨的人他都会杀。”

“你有长进。我们的话你说得好了，”泽霍瓦微微笑道，“用不了多久，你就能亲口告诉苏丹你爱他，说得同我一样好了。”

===Original===
“But you do not love him,” Almasta answered boldly.

Zehowah bent her brows so that they met between her eyes as the grip of a bow. Then Khaled’s heart leaped in his breast, for he saw that she was angry with the woman, and he supposed it was because she secretly loved him. But he held his breath lest even his breathing should betray him.

“The portion of fools is fire,” said Zehowah, not deigning to give any other answer. For she was a king’s daughter and Almasta a bought slave, though Khaled had taken her in war.

“Be merciful!” exclaimed Almasta, in humble tones. “I am your handmaid, and I speak Arabic badly.”

===Chinese===
“可你并不爱他，”阿尔玛斯塔大胆地答道。

泽霍瓦双眉紧锁，眉心拧成一个弓扣。哈立德的心在胸中猛跳，因为他看出她对这女人动了怒，而他猜想那是因为她心里暗暗爱着他。他屏住呼吸，生怕连呼吸声都会暴露自己。

“愚人的份是烈火，”泽霍瓦说，不屑再作别的回答。因为她是王的女儿，而阿尔玛斯塔不过是买来的奴隶，尽管是哈立德在战争中掳来的。

“发发慈悲吧！”阿尔玛斯塔低声下气地叫道，“我是您的侍女，阿拉伯话说得不好。”

===Original===
“You speak with exceeding clearness when it pleases you.”

“Indeed I cannot talk in your language, for it is not long since I came into Arabia.”

“We will have you taught, for we will give you a husband who will teach you with sticks. There is a certain hunchback, having one eye and marked with the smallpox, whose fists are as the feet of an old camel. He will be a good husband for you and will teach you the Arabic language, and your skin shall be dissolved but your mind will be enlightened thereby.”

“Be merciful! I desire no husband.”

===Chinese===
“你什么时候愿意，就说得清楚得很。”

“我真的说不好你们的话，我来阿拉伯没多久。”

“我们会请人教你，因为我们要给你一个用棍子教你的丈夫。有个驼背，独眼，一脸麻子，拳头像老骆驼的蹄子。他准是你的好丈夫，会教你阿拉伯话；你的皮要被打开花，脑子却会因此开窍。”

“发发慈悲吧！我不要丈夫。”

===Original===
“It is good that a woman should marry, even though the bridegroom be a hunchback. But if you will tell me your secret I will give you a better husband and forgive you.”

“There is no secret! I have killed no one!” cried Almasta. “Who has told you the lie?”

“And moreover,” continued Zehowah, not regarding her protestations, “there are other ways of learning secrets, besides by kindness; such, for instance, as sticks, and hot irons, and hunger and thirst in a prison where there are reptiles and poisonous spiders, besides many other things with which I have no doubt the slaves of the palace are acquainted. It is better that you should tell your secret and be happy.”

“There is no secret,” Almasta repeated, and she would say nothing else, for she did not trust Zehowah and feared a cruel death if she told the truth.

===Chinese===
“女人终归要嫁人的才好，哪怕新郎是个驼背。可你若把秘密告诉我，我就给你一个更好的丈夫，还饶恕你。”

“没有什么秘密！我没杀过人！”阿尔玛斯塔叫道，“这谎话是谁告诉你的？”

“再者，”泽霍瓦不理会她的分辩，继续说道，“除了好言好语，还有别的法子打听秘密；比如棍子，烧红的烙铁，还有牢里的饥渴——那牢里养着毒虫和蜘蛛，此外还有许多东西，不用说，宫里的奴隶们都是见识过的。你还是说出秘密、保全快活的好。”

“没有什么秘密，”阿尔玛斯塔重复道，此外什么也不肯说了，因为她信不过泽霍瓦，怕说了实话会死得残酷。

===Original===
