In the course of time therefore the blind man grew great in the eyes of his fellows, until they called him Sheikh respectfully, and waited on him when he performed his ablutions, and he obtained over them a supremacy as great as was Khaled’s over the kingdom he governed. He was very wise also, acquainted with the interpretation of dreams, and able to recite various chapters of the Koran. It was even said that he was able to distinguish a good man from a bad by the sound of his tread, though some thought that he only heard the jingling of coins in the girdle, and judged by this, having a finer hearing than other men. At all events he was often aware that a person able to give alms was approaching, while his companions were talking among themselves and noticed nothing, though they had eyes to see, being mostly only cripples and lepers.

On a certain day in the spring, when the sun was beginning to be hot and not long after Khaled had told Zehowah his story, many of the beggars were sitting in the eastern gate, by which the great road issues out of the city towards Hasa. They expected the coming of the first pilgrims every day, for the season was advancing. And now they sat talking together of the good prospects before them, and rejoicing that the winter was over so that they would not suffer any more from the cold.

“There is a horseman on the road,” said the Sheikh of the beggars, interrupting the conversation. “O you to whom Allah has preserved the light of day, look forth and tell me who the rider is.”

===Chinese===
乞丐们的谢赫是个老人，自幼失明，除此之外身强体健，性好争斗，抡起棍子来毫不含糊。他起初沦为乞丐时还年轻，因为父母双亡，没给他留下什么生计，他又是孤身一人，没有兄弟。由于他自夸两边都是纯正的沙漠血统，别的乞丐起初都讥笑他，嘲弄地称他作“伊本·谢赫（Ibn el Sheikh）”，即“谢赫之子”，有时还偷他的吃食。可他抡起棍子把众人痛打一顿——分不清好歹，一概照打——从此在众乞丐中挣得了极大的体面；此后他行事大方，把自己的一份同大伙合在一起充作公用，还常有添补。伙伴们后来也知道他的来历不假，他的血统从阿治曼（Ajman）到卡拉（El Kara）不输给任何人，因为有个与阿尔玛斯塔的丈夫阿卜杜拉同部落的贝都因人，每年来看他不下一次，唤他兄弟，还用大麦装满他的口袋。这贝都因人也是个有身份的人物，乞丐们从他自有一匹母马、配着好鞍、又有像样的兵器便看出来了。日子久了，这瞎子在同伴中间便渐渐显赫起来，直到众人都恭敬地称他谢赫，他行小净时还有人侍候，他在众人之上建立起来的威权，同哈立德在他统治的王国之上一样大。他还极有智慧，通晓圆梦，能诵《古兰经》里的好几章。甚至有人说，他能凭脚步声分辨好人坏人——虽说也有人以为，他不过是听见了腰带上铜钱相击的声音，凭这个判断，只是耳朵比常人灵些罢了。无论如何，每当一个肯施舍的人走近，同伴们还在彼此闲谈、毫无察觉——他们虽有眼睛，多半不过是些瘸子和麻风病人——他倒常常先知道了。

春天里的一天，日头开始转热，哈立德把自己的身世讲给泽霍瓦听后不久，许多乞丐正坐在东门，大路便从那道门出城，通向哈萨（Hasa）。他们天天盼着头一批朝觐的人到来，因为季节在往前赶。这时他们坐在一起，谈论着眼面前的好光景，欢喜冬天总算过去，再不用受冻了。

“路上来了个骑马的。”乞丐们的谢赫打断众人的谈话，说道，“安拉替你们保住白昼之光的诸位啊，望出去瞧瞧，告诉我那骑手是谁。”

===Original===
“It is undoubtedly a pilgrim,” answered a young beggar, who was a stranger but had found his way to Riad without legs, no man knew how.

“Ass of Egypt,” replied the Sheikh reprovingly, “do pilgrims ride at a full gallop upon steeds of pure blood? But though your eyes are open your ears are deaf with the sleep of stupidity from which there is no awakening. That is a good horse, ridden by a light rider. Truly a man must itch to be called Haji who gallops thus on the road to Mecca.”

Then the others looked, and at last one of them spoke, a hunchback having but one eye, but that one was keen.

“O Sheikh,” he said, “rejoice and praise Allah, for I think it is he whom you call your brother, who comes in from the desert to visit you.”

“If that is the case, I will indeed give thanks,” answered the blind man, “for there is little in my barley-sack, less in my wallet and nothing at all in my stomach. Allah is gracious and compassionate!”

===Chinese===
“准是个朝觐的。”一个年轻乞丐答道。他是个外乡人，却不知怎么靠没有双腿找到了里雅得来。

“埃及的蠢驴，”谢赫申斥道，“朝觐的人会骑着纯血的骏马全速飞奔吗？你的眼睛倒是睁着，耳朵却叫愚蠢的酣睡堵聋了，那种觉是睡不醒的。那是匹好马，马上骑着个轻骑手。说真的，在去麦加（Mecca）的大路上这样狂奔的人，必定是急着想被人称作哈吉。”

于是众人都张望，末了有一个人开了口——是个驼背，只有一只眼，那只眼却尖得很。

“谢赫啊，”他说，“您就欢喜吧，赞美安拉，我想那是您称作兄弟的那个人，从沙漠里进来看您了。”

“若真如此，我定要谢恩，”瞎子答道，“我大麦口袋里没剩多少，褡裢里更少，肚子里则什么都没有。安拉是仁慈怜悯的！”

===Original===
The hunchback’s eye had not deceived him, and before long the Bedouin dismounted at the gate and looked about until he saw the Sheikh of the beggars, who indeed had already risen to welcome him. When they had embraced the Bedouin led the blind man along in the shadow of the eastern wall until they were so far from the rest that they might freely talk without being overheard. Then they sat down together, and the mare stood waiting before them.

“O my brother,” the Bedouin began, “was not my mother the adopted daughter of your uncle, upon whom be peace? And have I not called you brother and filled your barley-sack from time to time these many years?”

“This is true,” answered the Sheikh of the beggars. “Allah will requite you with seventy thousand days of unspeakable bliss for every grain of barley you have caused to pass my teeth. ‘Be constant in prayer and in giving alms,’ says the holy book, ‘and you shall find with Allah all the good which you have sent before you, for your souls.’ And it is also said, ‘Give alms to your kindred, and to the poor and to orphans.’ I am also grateful for all you have done, and my gratitude grows as a palm tree in the garden of my soul which is irrigated by your charity.”

“It is well, my brother, it is well. I know the uprightness of your heart, and I have not ridden hither from the desert to count the treasure which may be in store for me in paradise. Allah knows the good, as well as the evil. I have come for another purpose. But tell me first, what is the news in the city? Are there no strange rumours afloat of late concerning Khaled the Sultan?”

===Chinese===
驼背的那只眼没有看错。不多时，那贝都因人便在城门下了马，四下张望，直到看见乞丐们的谢赫——其实谢赫早已起身迎他了。两人拥抱过后，贝都因人领着瞎子顺着东墙的阴影走，走到离众人足够远、说话不怕被人听见的地方，才一同坐下，那匹母马立在跟前等着。

“我的兄弟啊，”贝都因人开口道，“我母亲岂不是你叔父的养女——愿他平安？这许多年来，我岂不是唤你兄弟，时时把你的大麦口袋装满？”

“这是实话，”乞丐们的谢赫答道，“你送进我嘴里的每一粒大麦，安拉都要用七万天难以言说的福乐报答你。圣书上说：‘坚守礼拜和施舍，你们为灵魂预先寄存的善，都必在安拉那里寻见。’又说：‘当施舍给你的亲族、穷人和孤儿。’你做的一切我也感激，我的感激像一棵枣椰树，长在我灵魂的园子里，由你的慈善浇灌。”

“很好，我的兄弟，很好。我知道你心地端正。我从沙漠里骑马到此，不是为了清点天堂里给我存着多少财宝。善也安拉知道，恶也安拉知道。我另有来意。不过先告诉我，城里有什么消息？近来关于苏丹哈立德，可有什么奇怪的传言在流传？”

===Original===
“In each man’s soul there are two wells,” said the blind man. “The one is the spring of truth, the other is the fountain of lies.”

“You are wise and full of years,” said the Bedouin, “and I understand your caution, for I also am not very young. But here we must speak plainly, for the time is short in which to act. A sandstorm has darkened the eyes of the men of the desert and they are saying that Khaled is a Shiyah, a Persian and a robber, and that he must be overthrown and a man of our own people made king in his stead.”

“I have indeed heard such a rumour.”

“It is more than a rumour. The tribes are even now assembling towards Riad, and before many days are past the end will come. Abdullah is the chief mover in this. But with your help, my brother, we will make his plotting empty and his scheming fruitless as a twig of *ghada* stuck into the sand, which will neither strike root nor bear leaves.”

===Chinese===
“每个人灵魂里都有两口井，”瞎子说，“一口是真理之泉，一口是谎言之泉。”

“你有智慧，年岁也高，”贝都因人说，“你的谨慎我明白，我年纪也不小了。可眼下我们必须有话直说，因为能动手的时间不多了。一场沙暴迷了沙漠人的眼，他们正在说，哈立德是什叶派、是波斯人、是强盗，说必须把他推翻，立一个我们自己人做国王。”

“这样的传言，我确实听见过。”

“这不止是传言。各部落眼下正朝里雅得集结，不出几天就要见分晓。这件事的主谋是阿卜杜拉。可是有你帮忙，我的兄弟，我们要叫他的密谋落空，叫他的算计徒劳，像插进沙里的一截加达（*ghada*）树枝，既不生根，也不长叶。”

===Original===
When the Sheikh of the beggars heard that he was expected to give help in frustrating Abdullah’s plans he was troubled and much astonished.

“Shall the blind sheep go out and fight the lion?” he inquired tremulously.

“Even so,” replied the Bedouin unmoved, “and, moreover, without danger to himself. Hear me first. Abdullah and his tribe will encamp in the low hills, in a few days, as usual, but somewhat earlier than in other years, and a great number of other Bedouins will be in the neighbouring valleys at the same time. Then Abdullah will come into the city openly and go to his house with his wife and slaves, and during several days he will receive the visits of his friends and return them, and go to the palace and salute Khaled, as though nothing were about to happen. But in the meantime he will make everything ready, for it is his intention to go into the palace at night, disguised in a woman’s garment, with his wife, and they will slay Khaled in his sleep, and bind Zehowah, and distribute much treasure among the guards and slaves, and before morning the city will be full of Bedouins all ready to proclaim Abdullah Sultan. And you alone can prevent all this.”

But the blind man laughed in his beard.

===Chinese===
乞丐们的谢赫听说要他出力挫败阿卜杜拉的图谋，心里既慌又大为诧异。

“难道叫瞎眼的羊出去同狮子搏斗？”他颤声问道。

“正是如此，”贝都因人不动声色地答道，“而且他自己毫无危险。先听我说。不出几天，阿卜杜拉和他的部落就会照常在矮山里扎营，只是比往年早些，同时还有大批别的贝都因人聚在附近的谷地里。然后阿卜杜拉会大摇大摆进城，带着妻子和奴隶回自己宅子，头几天接待朋友、回访客人，还进宫拜见哈立德，好像什么事都不会发生。可暗中他会把一切备妥，因为他打算夜里穿着女人的衣裳、带着妻子混进王宫，趁哈立德熟睡杀了他，捆住泽霍瓦，把大量财宝分给卫兵和奴隶，天亮之前，城里就满是贝都因人，只等拥立阿卜杜拉为苏丹了。而这一切，只有你能阻止。”

可瞎子捋着胡子笑了。

===Original===
“This is a good jest!” he cried. “You have sought out a valiant warrior to stand between the Sultan and death! I am blind and old, and a beggar, and you would have me stand in the path of Abdullah and a thousand armed men. They would certainly laugh, as I do. Let me take with me a few lepers and the Egyptian jackass without legs, who has flown among us lately like a locust out of the clear air. Verily, their strength shall avail against the lances of the desert.”

“This is no jest, my brother,” answered the Bedouin, gravely. “Neither I, nor a hundred armed horsemen with me could do what you will do unhurt. But I will save Khaled. For in the battle of the pass before we came to Haïl last summer when I had an arrow in my right arm and a spear thrust in my side, certain dogs of Shammars encompassed me, and darkness was already descending upon my eyes when Khaled rode in like a whirlwind of scythes, and sent four of them to hell, where they are now drinking molten brass like thirsty camels. Then I swore by Allah that I would defend him in the hour of need.”

“Why do you not then lie in wait for Abdullah yourself and slay him as he passes you in the dark?”

“Is he not the sheikh of my tribe? How then can I lay a hand on him? But I have thought of this during many nights in my tent, and you alone can do what is needed.”

===Chinese===
“这玩笑开得好！”他叫道，“你们竟找来一位勇士，站在苏丹和死亡之间！我又瞎又老，还是个乞丐，你们却要我去挡阿卜杜拉和一千个带兵器的人的路。他们准会笑，就像我现在这样笑。让我带上几个麻风病人，还有那条没腿的埃及蠢驴——他新近像晴空里飞来的一只蝗虫似的落到我们中间——他们的力气定能敌过沙漠的长矛！”

“这不是玩笑，我的兄弟，”贝都因人正色答道，“我，或是跟我来的一百个带兵器的骑手，都不能毫发无伤地做成你将要做的事。可我要救哈立德。去年夏天，我们到哈伊勒之前在山口的那一仗，我右臂中了一箭，胁下挨了一矛，几条沙玛尔恶狗围住了我，黑暗已经罩上我的眼睛，这时哈立德像一阵镰刀旋风杀了进来，打发四个下了地狱——他们这会儿正在那儿学干渴的骆驼喝熔化的铜汁呢。那时我就凭安拉起誓，他危难之时我必护他。”

“那你自己为何不埋伏下来，等阿卜杜拉黑夜从你面前经过时杀了他？”

“他岂不是我部落的谢赫？我怎能对他下手？可我在帐篷里想了多少个夜晚，只有你能做成这事。”

===Original===
“Surely this is folly,” said the Sheikh of the beggars. “You have met a hot wind in the desert and your mind is unsettled by it. I pray you come with me into the city to my dwelling, and take some refreshment, or at least let me send to the well for a drink of water.”

“My head is cool and I am not thirsty, nor is the hot wind blowing at this time of year. Hear me. I will tell you how to save Khaled from destruction, and you shall receive more gold than you have dreamed of, and a house, and rich garments, and a young wife of a good family to comfort your old age. For the deed is easy and safe, but the reward will be great, and you alone can do the one and earn the other.”

“I perceive,” said the blind man, “that you are indeed in earnest, but I cannot understand what I can do. We know that Khaled is forewarned, for it is not many days since he summoned the chief men in Riad, with the Kadi, to the palace, and refused to tell them the name of his father, but said that if they attacked him he would kill as many of them as he could.”

“I did not know this,” answered the Bedouin. “But the knowledge does not change my plan. Now hear me. You are the Sheikh of all the beggars in Riad﻿—may Allah send you long life and much gain﻿—they are an army and you are a captain. Moreover the beggars are doubtless attached to Khaled by his generosity, and all of you say in your hearts that under Abdullah there may be more sticks and less barley for you.”

===Chinese===
“这实在是糊涂事了，”乞丐们的谢赫说，“你在沙漠里遇上了热风，脑子被它吹乱了。我求你随我进城到我住处用些点心，至少让我打发人去井里打点水来你喝。”

“我的头脑清凉，也不渴，这个时节也刮不了热风。听我说。我告诉你怎样把哈立德从毁灭里救出来，你将得到的金子超过你梦中所见，还有一座宅子、华美的衣裳，和一个好人家的年轻妻子安慰你的晚年。这桩事又容易又稳妥，报酬却大，只有你能做成这一件、挣得那一件。”

“我看得出，”瞎子说，“你当真是认真的，可我不明白我能做什么。我们知道哈立德已得了预警，因为没过几天前，他把里雅得的头人们连同教法官召进宫去，不肯说出他父亲的名字，只说他们若动手攻他，他能杀多少便杀多少。”

“这个我不知道，”贝都因人答道，“可知道了也不改我的主意。听我说。你是里雅得全城乞丐的谢赫﻿——愿安拉赐你长寿、多多进益﻿——他们是一支军队，而你是统领。再者，乞丐们承哈立德慷慨之恩，必然向着他，你们心里都在说，在阿卜杜拉治下，棍子或许更多，大麦或许更少。”

===Original===
“This is true. But then, my brother, it is otherwise with you, for you are of Abdullah’s tribe and will have honour and riches if he is made Sultan. How then is my advantage also yours?”

“And did not this Abdullah in the first place divorce with ignominy his second wife, who is my kinswoman, being the daughter of my father’s sister? And has he restored the dowry as the law commands? Truly his new wife is even now sitting upon my cousin’s carpet. And secondly Abdullah made himself sheikh unjustly, for our sheikh should be Abdul Kerim’s son.”

“Yet you accepted Abdullah and promised him allegiance.”

“Does the camel say to his driver: ‘I do not like to carry a load of barley, I would rather bear a basket of dates’? ‘Eat what you please in your tent, but dress as other men,’ says the proverb. Hear me, for I speak wisdom. Abdullah will come into the city and go to his house, intending to prepare the way for evil. And he will walk about the streets as usual, without attendants, both because he knows that the people are mostly with him, and also in order not to attract notice. Now Abdullah is the spring from which all this wickedness flows, he is the chief camel whom the others follow, the coal in the ashes by which the fire is kept alive, the head without which the body cannot live. Dry up the spring, therefore, let the chief camel fall into a pit suddenly, extinguish the coal, strike off the head. Let them ask in the morning: ‘Where is he?’ And let him not be found anywhere. Then the people will be amazed and will not know what to do, having no leader. This is for you to do, and it can easily be done.”

===Chinese===
“这倒是实话。可是，我的兄弟，你的情形就不同了，你属于阿卜杜拉的部落，他做了苏丹，你就有尊荣富贵。那你的好处怎么又成了我的好处呢？”

“再说，这阿卜杜拉头一件便休弃了他的二房妻子，休得极不体面——那是我父亲的姊妹之女，我的亲戚。他可曾按律法归还了嫁妆？说真的，他的新欢这会儿就坐在我表姐的地毯上。第二件，阿卜杜拉当上谢赫本就不义，因为我们部落的谢赫本该是阿卜杜勒·克里姆之子。”

“可你还是认了阿卜杜拉，向他效过忠。”

“骆驼可会对赶驼人说：‘我不爱驮大麦，倒想驮一筐椰枣’？谚语说得好：‘帐篷里随你吃什么，衣着上须随众人。’听我说，我讲的是至理。阿卜杜拉会进城回自己的宅子，预备为恶事铺路。他会同往常一样在街上游走，不带随从——一来他知道百姓多半向着他，二来也免得惹眼。如今阿卜杜拉便是这一切恶事的源头，是众驼跟随的头驼，是灰烬里养着火种的那块炭，是没有它身子便活不成的头颅。所以，塞住这泉眼，叫头驼猛地跌进陷坑，熄灭这块炭，砍下这颗头。明早让人们去问：‘他在哪儿？’叫他们哪里也寻不着他。百姓便会惊慌失措，没了头领，不知如何是好。这事由你来做，而且很容易做到。”

===Original===
“What folly is this?” asked the blind man, shaking his head. “And how can I do what you wish?”

“It is very easy, for I know that you and your companions are as one man, living together for the common good. Go to the beggars therefore and tell them what I have told you, and be not afraid, for they will not betray you. And when Abdullah walks about the city alone lie in wait for him, for you will easily catch him in a narrow street, and two or three score of you can run after him begging for alms, until he is surrounded on all sides. Then fall upon him, and bind him, and take him secretly to one of your dwellings and keep him there, so that none find him, until the storm is past. In this way you will save Khaled and the kingdom, and when all is quiet you can deliver him up to be a laughingstock at the palace and to all who believed in him. For there is nothing to fear, and I, for my part, am sure that Abdul Kerim’s son will immediately be made sheikh of our tribe so that Abdullah will not return to us.”

“You are subtle, my brother,” said the Sheikh of the beggars, smiling and stroking his beard. “This is a good plan, being very simple, and Khaled will be grateful to us, and honour us beggars exceedingly. Said I not well that the jest was good? Surely it is better than I had thought, and more profitable.”

“I have thought of it long in the nights of winter, both by the camp fire and in my tent and on the march. But I have told no one, nor will tell anyone until all is done. But so soon as you have taken Abdullah and hidden him, let me know of it. To this end, when we are encamped outside the city I will come every evening to prayers in the great mosque and afterwards will wait for you near the door. As soon as I know that Abdullah is out of finding I will spread the report that he is lost, and before long all our tribe will give up the search, being indeed glad to get rid of him. And the rest is in the hand of Allah. I have done what I can, you must now do your share.”

===Chinese===
“这算什么话？”瞎子摇着头问，“你要我做的事，我怎么能办到？”

“这容易得很，因为我知道你同你的伙伴们亲如一人，为着共同的利益过活。你只管去把我的话告诉众乞丐，不必害怕，他们不会出卖你。等阿卜杜拉独自在城里走动时，你们埋伏下来，在窄街上轻易就能截住他，你们二三十个人跟在他身后讨施舍，把他四面围住。然后扑上去，捆了他，悄悄带到你们哪一处住处藏起来，叫谁也寻不着，直等风头过去。这样你就救了哈立德和这个王国，等一切平静了，再把他交出去，让王宫里和所有信过他的人拿他取笑。这事没什么可怕的，而且我敢说，阿卜杜勒·克里姆之子立刻就会当上我们部落的谢赫，阿卜杜拉也就回不去了。”

“你可真够精明的，我的兄弟，”乞丐们的谢赫笑道，捋着胡子，“这是个好主意，好在简单，哈立德会感激我们，把我们乞丐抬举到十分。我方才不是说这玩笑开得好么？它实在比我起先想的还要好，还更有赚头。”

“整个冬天的夜里我都在想这件事，在营火边想，在帐篷里想，在行军路上想。可我一个字没告诉人，事成之前也不会告诉任何人。你一把阿卜杜拉捉住藏好，就设法让我知道。为这个缘故，我们在城外扎营之后，我每晚都去大清真寺做礼拜，礼拜完就在寺门附近等你。我一知道阿卜杜拉寻不着了，就散出他失踪的消息，要不了多久，我们全族都会放弃寻找——巴不得甩脱他呢。其余的就在安拉手中。我已尽了力，如今该你尽你的份了。”

===Original===
“By Allah! You shall not complain of me,” answered the blind man, “nor of my people, for the jest is surpassingly good, and shall be well carried out.”

“I will therefore go into the city, where I have business,” said the Bedouin. “For I gave a reason for coming alone to Riad, and must needs show myself there to those who know me.”

So the Bedouin filled the blind beggar’s sack with barley and dates from his own supply and embraced him and went into the city, but the Sheikh of the beggars remained sitting in the same place for some time, at a distance from the rest, in an attitude of inward contemplation, though he was in reality listening to what the hunchback was telling the new cripple from Egypt. The Sheikh’s ears were sharper than those of other men and he heard very clearly what was said.

“This Bedouin,” said the hunchback, “is a near relation of our Sheikh, and holds him in great veneration, coming frequently to see him even from a considerable distance, and always bringing him a present of food. And you may see by his mare and by his weapons that he is a person of consideration in his tribe. For our Sheikh is not a negro, nor the son of a Syrian camel-driver, but an Arab of the best blood in the desert, and wise enough to sit in the council in the Sultan’s palace. You, who are but lately arrived, being transported into our midst by the mercy of Allah, must learn all these things, and you will also find out that our Sheikh has eyes in his ears, and in his fingers and in his staff, though he is counted blind, and you cannot deceive him easily as you might suppose.”

===Chinese===
“凭安拉起誓！你不会埋怨我的，”瞎子答道，“也不会埋怨我的人马，这玩笑好得出奇，一定办得漂漂亮亮。”

“那么我就进城去了，城里我有事，”贝都因人说，“我独自来里雅得总得有个缘由，得到熟人面前露一露脸。”

于是贝都因人从自己的口粮里取出大麦和椰枣，装满瞎乞丐的口袋，拥抱了他，进城去了。乞丐们的谢赫却在原地又坐了一阵，离众人远远的，摆出一副内省沉思的模样，其实是在听驼背对新来的埃及瘸子讲些什么。谢赫的耳朵比常人尖，那番话他听得一清二楚。

“这个贝都因人，”驼背说，“是我们谢赫的近亲，极敬重他，隔得老远也常来看他，回回都带吃食作礼物。你从他那匹母马和他的兵器也看得出，他在部落里是个有头脸的人物。我们谢赫不是黑人，也不是叙利亚赶驼人的儿子，而是沙漠里最好血统的阿拉伯人，论智慧，坐进苏丹王宫的议事会也绰绰有余。你新来乍到，是蒙安拉的慈悲才落到我们中间的，这些事都得学会；你还会发现，我们谢赫耳朵里有眼睛，手指头里有眼睛，连棍子里也有眼睛——虽然人人算他瞎子——你想轻易哄他，可不像你以为的那么容易。”


===Original===
The Sheikh of the beggars was pleased when he heard this and listened attentively to hear the answer made by the Egyptian, whom he did not yet trust because he was a newcomer and a stranger.

“Truly,” replied the cripple, “Allah has been merciful and compassionate to me, for he has brought me into the society of the wise and the good, which is better than much feasting in the company of the ignorant and the ill-mannered. And as for the Sheikh, he is evidently a very holy man, to whom eyes are not in any way necessary, his inward sight being constantly fixed upon heavenly things.”

This answer did not altogether please the blind man, for it savoured somewhat of flattery. But the other beggars approved of the speech, deeming that it showed a submissive spirit, and readiness to obey and respect their chief.

“O you of Egypt!” cried the Sheikh, calling to him. “Come here and sit beside me, for I have heard what you said and desire your company.”

===Chinese===
乞丐们的谢赫听了这话心里受用，便凝神去听那埃及人怎么回答——他还没信过这人，因为他是新来的外乡人。

“真的，”瘸子答道，“安拉对我实在仁慈怜悯，把我带进了智者善人的圈子，这比同无知无礼之徒大吃大喝强多了。至于谢赫，他分明是位极圣洁的人，眼睛于他毫无必要，他内在的目光始终注视着天上的事。”

这个回答叫瞎子不十分受用，因为它有点阿谀的气味。可别的乞丐都夸这话好，觉得它显出一副恭顺的心性，乐于服从、敬重他们的首领。

“喂，埃及来的！”谢赫唤他道，“过来坐到我身边来，你的话我听见了，我想同你作伴。”

===Original===
The cripple immediately began to crawl along by the wall, dragging himself upon his hands and body, for he had no legs.

“He is obedient,” thought the blind man, “though it costs him much labour to move.”

When the man was beside him, the Sheikh took an onion and a date from his wallet and set them down upon the ground.

“Eat,” he said, “and give thanks.”

===Chinese===
瘸子立刻顺着墙根爬过来，靠双手和身子拖着自己走，因为他没有腿。

“他很恭顺，”瞎子心想，“虽然动一动要费好大的力气。”

那人爬到身边后，谢赫从褡裢里取出一个洋葱、一枚椰枣，放在地上。

“吃吧，”他说，“谢恩。”

===Original===
The cripple thanked him and taking the food, began to eat the onion.

“You have taken the onion in your right hand and the date in your left,” said the Sheikh. “And you are eating the onion first.”

“This is true,” answered the Egyptian. “I see that my lord has indeed eyes in his fingers.”

“I have,” said the Sheikh. “But that is not all, for this is an allegory. All men like to eat the onion first and the date afterwards, for though the onion be ever so sweet and tender, its taste is bitter when a man has eaten sugar-dates before it. But you have begun by giving us the mellow fruit of flattery, and when you give us the wholesome vegetable of truth it will be too sharp for our palates. Ponder this in your heart, chew it as the camel does her cud, and the well-digested food of wisdom shall nourish your understanding.”

===Chinese===
瘸子谢过了他，拿起食物，先吃起洋葱来。

“你右手拿的洋葱，左手拿的椰枣，”谢赫说，“而且你先吃洋葱。”

“正是，”埃及人答道，“我看主上手指头里当真长着眼睛。”

“是长着，”谢赫说，“可这还不算完，因为这里面有个比喻。人人都爱吃完了洋葱再吃椰枣，因为洋葱再甜再嫩，人先吃了蜜枣，再吃它便觉出苦味来。可你一开口先给了我们奉承这枚熟果，等你再给我们真理这棵健身的菜蔬时，我们上膛就要嫌它太辣了。这话你放在心里掂量，像骆驼倒嚼一般细细咀嚼，智慧这消化停当的食粮，便能滋养你的悟性。”

===Original===
The cripple listened in astonishment at the depth of the Sheikh’s thought, and he would have spoken out his admiration, but it is not possible to eat an onion and to be eloquent at the same time. The blind man knew this and continued to give him instruction.

“The onion has saved you,” he said, “for your mouth being full you could say nothing flattering, and now you will think before you speak. Consider how I have treated you. Have I at once rendered thanks to Allah for sending into our midst a young man whose gifts of eloquence are at least equal to those of the Kadi himself? I have said nothing so foolish. I have called you an ass of Egypt and otherwise rebuked you, for the good of your understanding, though I begin to think that you are indeed a very estimable young man, and it is possible that your wit may ripen in our society. But now I perceive by my hearing that you are eating the date. I pray you now, eat another onion after it.”

“I cannot,” answered the cripple, “for my lips are puckered at the thought of it.”

“Neither is truth sweet after flattery,” said the Sheikh, who then began to eat the other onion himself.

===Chinese===
瘸子听着，惊叹谢赫思想之深，本想把仰慕之情说出口来，可是嘴里嚼着洋葱，便没法子同时口若悬河。瞎子深知这一点，便接着教导他。

“这洋葱救了你，”他说，“你嘴里满满的说不出奉承话，往后你开口之前就先想一想了。瞧我是怎么待你的。我可曾立刻感谢安拉，谢他给我们中间送来一个口才至少不下于教法官本人的年轻人？我没说过这样的傻话。我叫你埃及蠢驴，还训斥了你，那是为了你的悟性好；不过我如今倒觉得，你确实是个很可敬的年轻人，你的机智在我们中间说不定会成熟起来。可现在我听出来了，你在吃椰枣。我请你，接着再吃一个洋葱。”

“吃不下了，”瘸子答道，“一想起来我的嘴就发苦发皱。”

“奉承过后，真理也一样不甜，”谢赫说，随即自己拿起另一个洋葱吃了起来。

===Original===
“I will endeavour to profit by your precepts, my lord,” replied the Egyptian.

“Allah will then certainly enlighten you, my son. Remember also another thing. We are ourselves here a community, distinct from the citizens of Riad, and what we do, we do for the common good. Remember therefore to share what you receive with the rest, as they will share what they have with you, and take part with them in whatsoever is done by common consent. In this way it will be well with you and you shall grow fat; but if you are against us you will find evil in every man’s hand, for since it has pleased Allah to give you no legs, you cannot possibly run away.”

Having said this much the Sheikh of the beggars was silent. But afterwards on the same day he gathered about him the strongest of his companions, being mostly men who had the use of both arms and both legs, though some of them were lepers and some had but one eye, and some were deaf and dumb, according to the affliction which it had pleased Allah to send upon each. These were the most trusty and faithful of his people, and to them he communicated openly what the Bedouin had proposed to him in secret. All of them approved the plan, for they greatly feared the overthrow of Khaled.

“But,” said one, “we cannot keep this Abdullah forever, and we can surely not kill him, for we should bring upon ourselves a grievous punishment.”

===Chinese===
“我一定努力领受主上的教诲。”埃及人答道。

“那样安拉必定开导你，我的孩子。还要记住一件事。我们自己在这里是一个会社，同里雅得的市民不相干，我们做的事，都是为着大家的好处。所以要记住，你得到的要分给众人，众人有的也会分给你；凡众人商量定的事，你都要一起做。这样你就顺顺当当，养得肥肥的；你若同我们作对，就会发现人人手里都攥着祸事等着你——安拉既高兴不给你腿，你可想跑也跑不掉。”

说完这些，乞丐们的谢赫便不再作声。当天晚些时候，他把同伴中最强壮的召集到身边——大都是四肢俱全的人，也有几个麻风病人，几个独眼的，几个又聋又哑的，全看安拉高兴把什么灾祸降到谁头上。这些是他手下最可靠最忠心的人，贝都因人私下向他提的主意，他公开告诉了他们。众人无不赞成，因为他们极怕哈立德被推翻。

“可是，”有一人说，“我们不能把阿卜杜拉永远关着，更不能杀了他，那会招来狠狠的惩罚。”

===Original===
“Allah forbid that we should shed blood,” replied the Sheikh. “But when Abdul Kerim’s son is made Sheikh of the tribe, Abdullah will probably not wish to go back to his people. Moreover it shall be for Khaled to judge what shall be done to the man, and he will probably cut off his head. But in the meantime it is necessary to choose amongst us spies, two for each gate of the city, to the number of twenty-two men, to watch for Abdullah. For we do not know when he will come, and of the two spies who see him enter, both must follow him and see whither he goes, and then the one will immediately inform all the rest while the other waits for him. From the time he enters the city he will not be able to go anywhere without our knowledge, and we shall certainly catch him one day towards dusk in some narrow street of the city.”

The beggars saw that this plan was wise and safe for themselves, and they did as the Sheikh advised, posting men at all the gates to wait for Abdullah. He was, indeed, not far distant, and before many days he rode into the city towards evening, attended by a few slaves and two Bedouins, his wife Almasta riding in the midst of them upon a camel. His face was not hidden and the two beggars who were watching recognised him immediately. They both followed him, until he entered his own house, and then the one sat down in the street to watch until he should come out, asking alms of those who accompanied him, until they also went in, with the beasts. But the other made haste to find the Sheikh and to inform him that Abdullah had come and was now in his own dwelling.

“It is well,” said the blind man. “The cat is now asleep, and dreams of mice, but he shall wake in the midst of dogs. Abdullah will not leave his house tonight, for it is late, and though he is not afraid in the daytime, he will not go out much at night, lest a secret messenger from Khaled, bearing evil in his hand, should meet him by the way. But tomorrow before dawn, some of us will wait in the neighbourhood of his house, and two or three score of others feigning to be all blind, as I am, must always be near at hand, watching us. We will then begin to importune him for alms, flattering him with fine language, as though we knew his plans. And this we will do continually, when he is abroad, until one day to escape from us he will turn quickly into a narrow street, supposing that we cannot see him. For he will not wish to be pursued by our cries in the bazaar lest he be obliged for shame to give something to each. Then those who can see will open their eyes and we will catch him in the lane, and bind rags over his head so that he cannot cry out, and lead him away to my dwelling by the Yemamah gate. And if any meet us by the way and inquire whom we are taking with us, we will say that he is one of ourselves, who is an epileptic and has fallen down in a fit, and that we are taking him to the farrier’s by the gate, to be burned with red-hot irons for his recovery, as the physicians recommend in such cases. Surely we have now foreseen most things, but if we have forgotten anything, Allah will doubtless provide.”

===Chinese===
“但愿安拉不许我们见血，”谢赫答道，“等阿卜杜勒·克里姆之子当了部落谢赫，阿卜杜拉多半就不想回他的人那里去了。况且这人的处置，自有哈立德来断，他多半会砍掉他的头。眼下先要从我们中间挑出探子，每座城门两个，共二十二个人，守候阿卜杜拉。我们不知道他几时来；望见他进城的两个探子都得跟着他，看他到哪里去，然后一个立刻去通知众人，另一个守在原地等他。从进城那一刻起，他走到哪里都逃不出我们的耳目，早晚有一天，我们会趁黄昏在城里哪条窄街上逮住他。”

乞丐们看这主意对自己又稳妥又聪明，便照谢赫说的办，在各座城门都派了人守候阿卜杜拉。他果然离得不远，没过几天，便在傍晚骑马进了城，跟着几个奴隶和两个贝都因人，他的妻子阿尔玛斯塔骑着一头骆驼走在他们中间。他没有蒙面，守门两个乞丐一眼便认出了他。两人都跟了上去，直到他进了自家的宅子；于是一个坐在街上看守，等他出来，又向随他来的人讨施舍，直讨到他们连同牲口也都进了门；另一个赶紧去找谢赫报信：阿卜杜拉来了，如今正在自己家里。

“这就好，”瞎子说，“猫如今睡着了，梦见耗子，可他要在狗群里醒来。阿卜杜拉今夜不会出他的宅子，天已晚了；他白天虽不怕，夜里却不常出门，生怕哈立德的密使手里攥着祸事，半路上迎着他。可明天天亮之前，我们几个人就在他宅子附近守着，另外二三十人装作和我一样全是瞎子，必须总在近旁看着我们。我们就缠着他讨施舍，用好话奉承他，仿佛我们识得他的计策。他出门在外，我们就日日如此，直到有一天他为了躲开我们，一头拐进窄街，以为我们看不见他——他不肯让我们一路叫喊着追进集市，免得臊得只好见人就赏。那时看得见的便睁开眼，我们在巷子里逮住他，用破布缠住他的头叫他喊不出声，把他带到我亚玛玛门边的住处去。路上若有人撞见，问我们带走的是谁，就说他是我们自己人，犯了羊癫疯倒在地上，我们正送他去城门边蹄铁匠那里，照医师们在这种情况下开的方子，用烧红的烙铁烙一烙，好叫他痊愈。如今我们多半都料到了；若还有什么没想到的，安拉必定补上。”

===Original===
All the beggars in council approved this plan, for they saw that it could be easily carried out, if they could only catch Abdullah in a lonely street at the hour of prayer when few persons are passing.

But Abdullah himself was ignorant of the evil in store for him, and feared nothing, having been secretly informed that most of the better sort of people were ready to support him if he would strike the blow; for they suspected Khaled of being a traitor, especially since he had last addressed the chief men and refused to tell the name of his father. Abdullah therefore came and went openly in the city.

In the meantime, however, Khaled was informed of his presence and was warned of the danger. The aged Kadi came secretly by night to the palace and desired to be received by the Sultan in order to communicate to him news of great importance, as he said. Khaled immediately received him, and the Kadi proceeded to give a full account of Abdullah’s designs; but the Sultan expressed no astonishment.

===Chinese===
议事的所有乞丐都赞成这个主意，因为他们看得出，只要能在礼拜时辰行人稀少的时候，把阿卜杜拉截在僻静的街上，这事便容易办成。

可阿卜杜拉本人全然不知大祸等着他，什么都不怕，因为已有密信告诉他，只要他肯下手，大多数上等人家都愿意帮他；他们疑心哈立德是奸细，尤其是他上次召见头人们、却不肯说出父亲名字之后。于是阿卜杜拉在城里来来去去，毫不遮掩。

然而这时，哈立德也得到了他到来的消息，有人向他示了警。年迈的教法官夜里悄悄来到王宫，求见苏丹，说有机密大事相告。哈立德立刻接见了他，教法官便把阿卜杜拉的图谋一五一十说了；可苏丹毫无惊讶之色。

===Original===
“Let him do what he will,” he answered, “for I care little and, after all, what must be will be.”

“But I beseech you to consider,” said the Kadi, “that by acting promptly you could easily quell this revolution, in which I, by Allah, have no part and will have none. For though many persons may just now desire your overthrow, because they expect to get a share of the treasure in the confusion, yet few are disposed to accept such a man as Abdullah ibn Mohammed el Herir in your place. Even his own tribe are not all faithful to him, and I am credibly informed that many look upon him as an intruder, and would prefer the son of Abdul Kerim for sheikh, as would be just, if the rights of birth were considered. And it would be an easy matter to remove this Abdullah. I implore you to think of the matter.”

“Would this not be a murder?” asked Khaled, looking curiously at the venerable preacher.

“Allah is merciful and forgiving,” replied the old man, looking down and stroking his beard. “And moreover, if you suffer Abdullah to go about a few days longer he will certainly destroy you, whereas it is an easy matter to give him a cup of such good drink as will save him from thirst ever afterwards, and you would obtain quiet and the kingdom would be at peace.”

===Chinese===
“随他做什么去吧，”他答道，“我不大在乎，再说，该来的总要来。”

“可我求您想一想，”教法官说，“只要当机立断，这场变乱轻易便能平定——我凭安拉起誓，我在里头没有份，也绝不会有份。眼下虽然许多人盼着您倒台，指望乱中分得一份财宝，可愿意让阿卜杜拉·伊本·穆罕默德·埃尔赫里尔（Abdullah ibn Mohammed el Herir）这样一个人顶替您的，却没有几个。连他本部落的人也未必都忠于他，我得到可靠的消息，许多人拿他当僭位的闯入者，宁要阿卜杜勒·克里姆之子做谢赫——若论出身名分，这本也是正理。除掉这个阿卜杜拉，是件容易的事。我恳求您把这事想一想。”

“这岂不是杀人？”哈立德问，好奇地望着这位可敬的讲道人。

“安拉是仁慈宽恕的，”老人答道，垂下眼，捋着胡须，“再者，您若放任阿卜杜拉再走动几天，他必定毁掉您；而请他喝上一杯好饮料，叫他从此永远不再口渴，却是件容易的事，您得了安宁，王国也享太平。”

===Original===
“They shall not find me sleeping,” said Khaled, “and so that I may only slay a score of them first, I care not how soon I perish.”

“This is indeed a new kind of madness!” exclaimed the Kadi. “I cannot understand it. But I have done what I could, and I can do nothing more.”

“Nor is there anything more to be done,” said Khaled. “But I thank you, for it is clear that you have spoken from a good intention.”

So the Kadi went away again, and Khaled returned to Zehowah, caring not at all whether he lived or died. But Zehowah began to watch him narrowly.

“If this man were a Persian, an enemy and a traitor,” she thought, “he would now begin to take measures for his own safety, seeing that he is threatened on every side. Yet he does not lift a hand to defend himself. This can proceed only from one of two causes. Either he is a Djinn, as he has told me, and they cannot kill him, and so he does not fear them; or else he desires death, out of a sort of madness which has grown up in him through this love of which he is always speaking.”

===Chinese===
“他们不会发现我在酣睡，”哈立德说，“只要能先宰掉他们二十个，我自己多早晚完蛋，我都不在乎。”

“这真是一种新式的疯病！”教法官叫道，“我弄不明白。可我已尽了力，再无能为力了。”

“本来也没有什么可做的了，”哈立德说，“不过我谢谢你，你分明是出于好意才说的。”

于是教法官走了，哈立德回到泽霍瓦身边，全不把自己的死活放在心上。泽霍瓦却开始细细地留心观察他。

“这人若真是个波斯人、是个仇敌和奸细，”她心想，“如今四面遭人威胁，他总该着手保全自己了。可他连一根手指也不抬起来自卫。这只能出于两个缘故：要么他真是巨灵，像他自己说的那样，他们杀不死他，所以他不怕；要么他就是求死，从他常挂在嘴边的这场爱里，长出了一种疯病。”

## XI / 十一

===Original===
In these days many of the Bedouin tribes came near the city and encamped in great numbers within half a day’s journey and less. Abdullah was exceedingly busy with his preparations, and spent much time in talking with other sheikhs, hardly making any concealment of his movements or plans. For by this time it seemed clear to him that the greater part of the people were with him, and everyone spoke of the coming overthrow of Khaled as an open matter. Khaled himself, too, was reported to be in fear of his life, and he was no longer seen in the streets as formerly, nor in the courts of the palace, nor even every day in the hall, but remained shut up in the harem, and none saw him except the women and a few slaves. Men said aloud that he was in great fear and distress, and as this story gained credence, so Abdullah’s importance increased, since it was he who had brought such terror upon Khaled. All this was open talk in the bazaar, but Abdullah was himself somewhat suspicious, supposing that Khaled must have a plan in reserve for defending his possession of the throne. Abdullah, however, kept secret the manner in which he intended to enter the palace, though he promised his adherents to open to them the gates of the castle, and the doors of the treasure chambers on a certain day, which he named, at the time of the first call to prayer in the morning, warning all those who were with him to come together in the great square before that hour in order to be ready to help him, if necessary, and to overwhelm the guards of the palace if they should make any resistance. But he did not know that the man of his tribe who was kinsman to the chief of the beggars had overheard his talk with his wife.

Meanwhile the beggars seemed to be multiplied exceedingly in Riad, for whenever Abdullah went out of his house they came upon him, sometimes by twos and threes and sometimes in scores, pressing close to him and begging alms. They also cried out a great deal, praising his generosity and praying for blessings upon him.

===Chinese===
这些日子里，许多贝都因部落逼近城边，大批人马在半日乃至更近的路程之内扎下营来。阿卜杜拉忙着各项准备，把许多时间花在同别的谢赫攀谈上，言行计划几乎不加遮掩。因为这时在他看来，大部分百姓都已站在他一边，人人都把哈立德即将倒台当作公开的事来谈论。据说哈立德本人也怕性命难保，街面上不再像从前那样见得到他，宫院里见不到，连正殿里也不是天天露面，只闭门守在内宫，除了女人和几个奴隶谁也见不着他。人们高声说他惶恐万分，这说法越传越可信，阿卜杜拉的声势便随之看涨，因为叫哈立德闻风丧胆的正是他。这些话在集市上公开流传，可阿卜杜拉自己心里还有几分疑忌，料定哈立德必留着什么保住王位的后手。不过阿卜杜拉把进宫的法子秘而不宣，只向党羽们许诺，到了他指定的那一日，晨祷头一声召唤的时辰，他会为他们打开城堡的大门和宝库的房门；他告诫所有同伙务必在那时刻之前聚到大广场上，以便必要时接应他，倘若王宫卫兵抵抗，便将他们一举踏平。但他不知道，他部落里那个与乞丐头领沾亲的人，早已把他同妻子的谈话听了个正着。

与此同时，里雅得的乞丐似乎陡然多了起来，因为阿卜杜拉一出门，他们便扑上来，有时三三两两，有时成群结队，紧紧围着他讨施舍，还高声喧嚷，夸他慷慨，为他求福。

===Original===
“Behold the sheikh of sheikhs!” they exclaimed. “He bears gold in his right hand and silver in his left. *Yallah!* Send him a long life and prosperity, for he loves the poor and his name is the Almsgiver. He is not El Herir but Er Rahman and his heart overflows with mercy as his purse does with small coins. Come, O brothers, and taste of his charity, which is a perpetual spring of good water beside a palm tree full of sugar-dates! Ya Abdullah, Servant of Allah, we love you! You are our father and mother. Your kefiyeh is the banner which goes before our pilgrimage. Come, O brothers, and taste of his charity.”

Abdullah was not dissatisfied with these words, and the beggars said much more to the same effect, which he regarded as signs of his popularity, so that he opened his purse from time to time and threw handfuls of money into the crowd, not counting the cost since he expected to be master of all the treasure in Riad within a few days. But the beggars were disappointed, for they had hoped that he would turn out to be avaricious, and endeavour to elude them by walking through narrow and lonely streets, where they might catch him. So they pressed more and more upon him every day, trying to exhaust his patience and his charity. In this however they failed, not understanding that the vanity of such a man is inexhaustible and knows no price. Abdullah, too, chose rather to be abroad during the daytime than in the evening or the early morning, for he desired to be seen by the multitude and spoken of as he went through the marketplace. Yet on the last evening of all he fell into the hands of the Sheikh of the beggars, and evil befell him.

The hour of prayer was passed and it was almost the time when lights are extinguished. Then Abdullah took his sword under his aba, and also a good knife, which he had proved in battle, and which in his hand would pierce a coat of mail as though it were silk. Almasta, his wife, also made a bundle of woman’s clothing and carried it in her arms. For they intended to go to a lonely place by the city wall, that Abdullah might there put on female garments, before entering the palace. He feared, indeed, lest if it were afterwards known by what disguise he had accomplished his purpose, he might receive some name in derision, from which he should never escape so long as he lived. Yet he had no choice but to dress as a woman, since he could not otherwise by any means have gone into the harem.

===Chinese===
“看哪，谢赫中的谢赫！”他们喊道，“他右手擎着金子，左手捧着银子。*Yallah*！求安拉赐他长寿兴旺，因为他爱护穷人，他的名号便是大施主。他不是‘埃尔赫里尔’，分明是‘拉赫曼’（Er Rahman）——仁慈从他心里满溢，就像小钱从他钱袋里满得往外洒。来呀，兄弟们，尝尝他慈善的味道，那是一棵结满蜜枣的椰枣树旁一口常流的甘泉！呀，阿卜杜拉，安拉的仆人，我们爱你！你是我们的爹娘。你的卡菲耶（kefiyeh）头巾，就是我们朝觐路上的前导旗。来呀，兄弟们，尝尝他慈善的味道。”

这些话叫阿卜杜拉听着受用。乞丐们还说了许多大同小异的好话，他都当做人望的凭据，于是不时解开钱袋，成把地把钱撒进人群，也不计较破费——反正不出几天，里雅得的全部财宝就都是他的了。乞丐们却大失所望，他们原盼着他是个吝啬鬼，会设法钻进偏僻窄巷甩开他们，好叫他们下手。于是他们一天比一天缠得更紧，想耗尽他的耐心和善心。这一着却落了空，他们不明白，这种人的虚荣心是用不完的，不识价钱。阿卜杜拉也宁可白天在外走动，不拣傍晚或清晨，因为他愿意让大众看见，听人议论他走过集市。然而到末了的那个晚上，他还是落进了乞丐谢赫的手里，祸事找上了他。

礼拜的时辰已过，差不多到了熄灯的时候。阿卜杜拉把剑藏进阿巴（aba）斗篷底下，还带了一把好刀，那是战场上验证过的，在他手里能穿透锁子甲，如同穿透丝绸。他的妻子阿尔玛斯塔也打了个衣裳包袱抱在怀里。因为他们打算先到城墙边一处僻静地方，让阿卜杜拉在那里换上女人衣裳，再进王宫。他实在怕的是，日后若被人知道他靠这等装扮成事，会得一个洗不掉的嘲笑名号，一辈子甩不脱。可他又不能不扮作女人，否则无论如何进不了内宫。

===Original===
As he came out of his house, accompanied only by Almasta he was seen at once by the two beggars who were always on the watch. And then, wishing to warn their companions, of whom many were lying asleep upon doorsteps in the same street and in others close by, these two made haste to get up, pretending to be lame and making a great clatter with their staves, as they limped after Abdullah. Then he, who loved to exercise charity in the marketplace, but not in the dark where none could applaud him, made a pretence of not seeing the poor men, and went swiftly on with Almasta running by his side. But as he walked fast, the two beggars although apparently lame increased their speed with his, and their clatter also.

“Does a sound man need a horse to escape from cripples?” asked Abdullah. And he turned quickly into a narrow lane.

“It will be wiser to scatter a few coins to them,” said Almasta. “They will then stop and search for them in the dark. For these men are very importunate and will certainly hinder us.”

But Abdullah was confident in his legs as a strong man and only walked the faster, so that Almasta could with great difficulty keep beside him. Then they heard the beggars running after them in the dark and calling upon them.

===Chinese===
他出得门来，只有阿尔玛斯塔跟着，立刻被那两个常年守望的乞丐瞧在眼里。这两人要给同伴们报信——同一条街上和邻近街上有许多乞丐正睡在人家门阶上——便急忙爬起来，装成瘸子，抡着手杖一片乱响，一瘸一拐地跟在阿卜杜拉身后。他这个人在集市上乐善好施，可在黑地里没人喝彩的地方就不肯行了，便装作没瞧见这两个穷人，脚下飞快，阿尔玛斯塔小跑着才跟得上。可他走得越快，那两个明明瘸腿的乞丐也跟着加快，手杖敲得也越急。

“健全人逃命难道还得骑马，就为躲几个瘸子？”阿卜杜拉说着，急急拐进一条窄巷。

“撒几个钱给他们更聪明，”阿尔玛斯塔说，“他们就会停下来在黑地里摸钱。这些人缠人得很，定要坏我们的事。”

可阿卜杜拉凭着自己两条壮汉的腿，只是越走越快，阿尔玛斯塔好容易才跟在他身边。这时他们听见乞丐们在黑暗里追着喊。

===Original===
“O Abdullah!” they cried. “The light of your charitable countenance goes before us like a lantern, and illuminates the whole street! Be merciful and give us a small coin, and Allah will reward you!”

Then Abdullah stopped in the darkest part of the narrow lane, seeing that they had recognised him, and conceiving that it would be a reproach for a sheikh of pure blood to run from beggars; and he feared also that it would be remembered against him on the morrow. He therefore made a pretence of being diverted, and laughed.

“Surely,” he said, “the lame men of Riad could outrun in a race the sound men of any other city. And, by Allah, I have little money with me, for I was going to a friend’s house to receive a sum due to me for certain mares; yet I will give you what I have, and I pray you, go in peace.”

Thereupon he sought in his wallet for something to give them, and while he was seeking they began to praise him after their manner.

===Chinese===
“阿卜杜拉啊！”他们喊道，“您那慈祥面容的光辉像灯笼一样照在我们前头，把整条街都照亮了！发发慈悲，赏我们一个小钱吧，安拉必报答您！”

阿卜杜拉在窄巷最黑的地方站住了，见他们认出了自己，心想纯血统的谢赫被乞丐追着跑，传出去是桩耻辱，又怕明日被人口口相传。于是他装出被逗乐的样子，笑了。

“说真的，”他说，“里雅得的瘸子赛起跑来，能胜过任何别处的健全人。凭安拉起誓，我身上没带多少钱，我正要去朋友家收一笔卖马的款子；可我有的都给你们，请你们平安去吧。”

于是他在褡裢里摸要给的东西，他正摸着，他们照自己的规矩开始颂扬他。

===Original===
“See this Abdullah!” they said. “He is the father of the poor and distressed, and is ever ready to divide all he has with us. *Yallah!* Bless him exceedingly! *Yallah!* Increase his family!”

But when Abdullah had found the money and was putting it into their hands, he was suddenly aware that instead of two beggars there were now ten or more, and these again multiplied in an extraordinary manner, so that he felt himself hemmed in on every side in a close press.

“O Allah!” he exclaimed. “Thou art witness that unless these small coins are multiplied a hundredfold, as the basket of dates by the Prophet at the trench before Medina, I shall have nothing to give these worthy persons.”

By this time the blind Sheikh of the beggars was present, and he pushed forward, pretending to rebuke his companions.

===Chinese===
“瞧瞧这位阿卜杜拉！”他们说，“他是穷苦人的父亲，随时甘愿把所有的一切同我们平分。*Yallah*！大大地赐福给他！*Yallah*！叫他子孙满堂！”

可阿卜杜拉把钱摸出来往他们手里放的时候，忽然发觉乞丐已经不是两个，而是十个还多，而且还在异乎寻常地成倍增加，直挤得他四面八方团团围住，动弹不得。

“安拉啊！”他叫道，“你作证，除非这些小钱能像先知当年在麦地那（Medina）城壕前那筐椰枣一样翻上一百倍，我可拿什么给这些贵人们呢。”

这时乞丐们的瞎谢赫已经到场，他挤上前来，装模作样地申斥同伴。

===Original===
“O you greedy ones!” he cried. “How often have I told you not to be so importunate? Yet you crowd upon him like wasps upon a date, presuming upon the goodness of his heart, and when there is no more room you crowd upon each other. Forgive them, O Abdullah!” he said, addressing him directly, “for they have the appetites of jackals together with the understanding of little children. They would thrust into the dish a hand as small as a crow’s foot and withdraw it looking as big as a camel’s hoof. Their manners are also﻿—”

“My friend,” said Abdullah, “I have given what I can. Let me therefore pass on, for my business is of importance, yet the throng is so great that I cannot move a step. Tomorrow I will distribute much alms to you all.”

“The radiance of your merciful countenance is enough for us,” replied the Sheikh of the beggars, “and even I who am blind am comforted by its rays as by those of the sun in spring, and my hunger is appeased by the honey of your incomparable eloquence﻿—”

“My friend,” said Abdullah, interrupting him again, “I pray you to let me go forward now, for I have a very important matter in hand, though it is with difficulty that I tear myself away from your society and I would willingly listen much longer to the words of the wise.”

===Chinese===
“你们这些贪心的！”他叫道，“我说过多少回，叫你们别这样死缠？可你们围着他，像黄蜂叮着一枚椰枣，仗着他心肠好，挤不下了就互相挤。饶了他们吧，阿卜杜拉！”他转脸对他说，“因为他们长着豺狼的胃口，却只有小孩子的悟性。他们伸进盘里的手小得像乌鸦爪子，抽出来时瞧着倒有骆驼蹄子那么大。他们的举止也是﻿——”

“我的朋友，”阿卜杜拉说，“能给的我都给了。请放我过去吧，我有要紧的事，可人围得这样密，我一步也挪不动。明天我大大地布施给你们众人。”

“您仁慈面容的光辉于我们足矣，”乞丐们的谢赫答道，“连我这个瞎子也叫它的光芒暖着了，像春天的太阳照着一般，我肚子里的饿也叫您那无与伦比的口才之蜜喂饱了﻿——”

“我的朋友，”阿卜杜拉又打断他，“求你现在放我往前去吧，我手头有十分要紧的大事，虽然离开您的陪伴真叫人难舍，智者的话我本愿意再多听上一阵。”

===Original===
Then the blind man turned to the other beggars, and his hearing told him that by this time there were at least threescore in the street.

“Come, my brothers!” he cried. “Let us accompany our benefactor to the house of his friend, and afterwards we will wait for him and see that he reaches his own dwelling in safety. Surely it is not fitting that a sheikh of such great consideration should go about the streets at night without so much as an attendant carrying a lantern. Let us go with him.”

Now these last words were the signal agreed upon, and even as Abdullah began to protest that he desired no such honourable escort as the beggars offered him, one came from behind and suddenly drew a thick barley-sack over his head, so that his voice was heard no more, and he was dragged down by the throat, while the one-eyed hunchback caught him by the legs and bound his feet and four others laid hold of his hands and tied them firmly behind him. Nor had Almasta time to utter a single cry before she was bound hand and foot with her head in a sack, like her husband. Then at a signal the beggars took up the two as though they had been bales packed ready for a camel’s back, and carried them away swiftly into the darkness, towards the eastern gate where the blind man lived in a ruined house together with three or four of his most trusted companions. He also sent a messenger to his relation, the Bedouin, as had been agreed. It was already quite dark in the streets and the few persons who met the beggars did not see what they were carrying, nor ask questions of them, merely supposing that they had lingered long in the public square after evening prayers and were now returning in a body to their own quarter.

The blind man’s house was built of three rooms and a wall, standing in a square around a small court. But only one of the rooms had a roof of its own, though there was a sort of cellar under the floor of one of the others which served at once as a lodging for beggars in winter, as a storehouse for food when there was any in supply and as a place of deposit for the ancient iron chest in which the common fund of money was kept. To this vault the Sheikh of the beggars made his companions bring the two prisoners, and having set them on the floor, side by side, he proceeded to hold a council, in which the captives themselves had no part, since their heads were tied up in dusty barley-sacks and they could not speak so as to be heard.

===Chinese===
瞎子于是转向众乞丐，他的耳朵告诉他，这时街上至少已有六十来人。

“来吧，我的兄弟们！”他喊道，“我们送送这位恩主，送他到朋友家去，过后再候着他，保他平平安安回自己宅子。这么有身份的谢赫，夜里在街上走动，连个提灯笼的随从都不带，成何体统。我们陪他同去。”

这末几句话便是约好的暗号。阿卜杜拉刚要推辞，说不敢劳乞丐们这般隆重的护送，就有一人从背后闪出，冷不防把一条厚厚的大麦口袋套上他的头，他的声音便再听不见了，喉咙又被人掐住按倒，独眼驼背抓住他的双腿捆住双脚，另有四人捉住他的双手，牢牢反绑在背后。阿尔玛斯塔连一声都没来得及喊，就被捆了手脚，头上套了口袋，同她丈夫一样。随后一声暗号，乞丐们扛起这两个人，像扛两包给骆驼备好的货驮，飞快地抬进黑暗里，直奔东门——瞎子同三四个最亲信的同伴就住在那边一座破屋里。他还按约定派人给他的亲戚、那贝都因人送了信。街上已经全黑，偶尔撞见乞丐的人也看不清他们抬着什么，更不来盘问，只当他们晚祷后在大广场上流连到此刻，正结伙回自己那片住处去。

瞎子的房子有三间屋、一面围墙，围成方方一个小院。只是其中一间有自己的屋顶，不过另一间屋子地板底下有个地窖模样之处，一物三用：冬天给乞丐栖身，有存粮时当粮仓，还搁着那口老铁箱，装着大伙公用的钱。乞丐们的谢赫叫同伴把两个俘虏弄到这地窖里，让他们并排坐在地上，随后便开庭议事，两个俘虏自己倒没有份，因为他们的头还裹在满是灰的大麦口袋里，说话也听不见。

===Original===
“O my brothers!” said the blind man. “Allah has delivered the enemies of the kingdom into our hand, and it is necessary to decide what we will do with them. Let the oldest and the wisest give their opinions first, and after them the others, even to the youngest, and last of all I will speak, and let us see whether we can agree.”

“Let us kill the man and bury him, and then cast lots among us for the woman,” said one.

“No,” said the next, a man who had twice made the pilgrimage, and was much respected, “we cannot do this, for the man is a true believer, and evil will befall us if we shed his blood. Let us rather keep him here, and purify his hide every day with our staves, until Khaled is in no more danger, and then we will take him to the palace and deliver him up.”

“It is to be feared,” said the Sheikh of the beggars, “that the man might chance to die of this sort of purification, though indeed it be very wholesome for him, and I am not altogether against it.”

===Chinese===
“我的兄弟们！”瞎子说，“安拉把王国的仇敌交到了我们手里，得商量个处置的法子。让年最长、智慧最高的先说，随后众人依次发言，直到最年轻的，最后我来讲，看我们能不能议到一处。”

“把男人杀了埋掉，然后咱们抓阄分那个女人。”有一人说。

“不行，”第二个说。这人朝觐过两回，颇受众敬重。“这可使不得，这人是个真信士，我们若流他的血，灾祸要落在我们头上。不如把他关在这儿，天天用棍子净化他的皮，直到哈立德再无危险，再押去王宫交出去。”

“只怕这人碰巧会叫这种净化法弄死，”乞丐们的谢赫说，“虽然那法子于他确实大有益处，我也不完全反对。”

===Original===
“Let us make him our slave,” said a third who had himself been the slave of a poor man who had died without heirs. “The fellow is strong. Let us buy millstones and make him grind barley for us in this cellar. In this way he will not eat our food for nothing.”

After this many others gave advice of the same kind. But while they were talking there was a great clattering and noise upon the stone steps which led down into the cellar, and a man fell over the last step and rolled over and over into the very midst of the council, railing and lamenting.

“It is that ass of Egypt,” said the Sheikh of the beggars. “I know him by the clattering of the wooden hoofs he wears on his hands, and also by his braying. Let him also give his opinion when he is recovered from his fall.”

“It is strange and marvellous,” said one, “that he who has no legs should suffer so many falls, being, by the will of Allah, always upon the Earth. For when we first saw him we found him fainting upon the ground, having fallen from the wall of a garden, though no man could tell how he had climbed upon it.”

===Chinese===
“叫他做咱们的奴隶吧。”第三个说，他自己从前给一个身后无嗣的穷人当过奴隶。“这汉子身强力壮。咱们买两扇磨来，叫他在地窖里给咱们磨大麦。这样他吃咱们的饭也不算白吃。”

此后又有许多人出了大同小异的主意。正说着，通往地窖的石阶上一阵乱响，一个人绊上最后一级台阶，骨碌碌一直滚到议事场的正当中，嘴里骂骂咧咧，叫苦连天。

“是埃及那头蠢驴，”乞丐们的谢赫说，“我听得出他手上套的木蹄子的响声，也听得出他的叫唤。等他从跌跤里缓过来，也叫他出个主意。”

“真是怪事一桩，”有人说，“他没有腿，却摔了这么多跤，凭安拉的旨意，他总归在地上。我们头一回见他，就见他在地上晕着——从一座园子的墙头上摔下来的，可谁也说不清他是怎么爬上去的。”

===Original===
“I had been transported to the top of the wall as in a dream,” replied the cripple, “for there were dates in that garden. But having eaten too greedily of them I fell asleep on the top and I dreamed that my body was torn by hyenas; and waking suddenly I fell down. For the dates were yet green.”

“This may or may not be true,” said the blind man. “For you are an Egyptian. Let us, however, hear what you have to advise in the matter of Abdullah and his wife, whom we have taken prisoners.”

“I fear that you mock me, O my lord,” answered the man. “But if I am mocked, I will advise that this Abdullah be also made a sport of, for us first, and for the people of Riad afterwards.”

“Tell us how this may be done, for a good jest is better than salt for roasting, and the sheep lie here bound before us.”

===Chinese===
“我是一阵迷糊才上到墙头的，像做梦一样，”瘸子答道，“因为那园子里有椰枣。可我吃得太贪，就在墙顶上睡着了，梦见身子叫鬣狗撕扯；猛然惊醒，便摔了下来。因为那枣还是青的。”

“这话可真可假，”瞎子说，“谁叫你是个埃及人。不过且听听，我们拿了阿卜杜拉和他妻子这两个俘虏，你有什么主意。”

“我怕主上是在取笑我，”那人答道，“可若说取笑，我出的主意也叫这阿卜杜拉成为笑柄——先笑在我们，后笑在里雅得众人。”

“说说怎么个做法，好玩笑胜过烤肉用的盐，羊就捆在这儿躺在我们面前呢。”


===Original===
“Take this man, then,” said the cripple, “and uncover his face, and hold him fast. Then let one of us get the razor and shave off all his beard and his eyebrows, and the hair of his head even to the nape of his neck. Then if he came suddenly before her who bore him and cried, ‘Mother,’ she would cover her face and answer, ‘Begone, thou ostrich’s egg!’ For she would not know him. And tomorrow we will take his excellent clothes from him and put them upon our Sheikh. But we will dress Abdullah in rags such as would not serve to wipe the mud from a slave’s shoes in the time of the subsiding waters, and we will tie his hands under his armpits and put a halter over his head and lead him about the city. Then he will cry out against us to the people, saying that he is Abdullah, but we will also cry out in answer: ‘See this madman, who believes himself to be a sheikh of Bedouins though Allah has given him no beard! O people of Riad, you may know that the spring is come, by the braying of this ass.’ ”

“Yet I see now that there may be wisdom in brayings,” said the Sheikh of the beggars, “though Balaam ibn Beor shut his ears against it, and was punished for his cursing so that his tongue hung down to his breast, all his days, like that of a thirsty dog. This is good counsel, for in this way we shall not shed the man’s blood, nor render ourselves guilty of his death; but I think we shall earn a great reward from Khaled, and his kingdom will be saved in laughter.”

During all this time Abdullah had not moved, knowing that he was in the power of many enemies and beyond all reach of help, but when he heard the decision of the Sheikh of the beggars he was filled with shame and rolled himself from side to side upon the floor, as though trying to escape from the bonds that held him. Almasta, for her part, lay quietly where they had put her, for she saw that all chance of success was gone and was pondering how she might take advantage of what happened, to save herself.

===Chinese===
“那就把这人提过来，”瘸子说，“揭开他的脸，按牢了。然后叫我们中间一个取剃刀来，把他的胡子眉毛统统剃光，头发一直剃到后颈。往后他冷不丁跑到生养他的母亲面前喊一声‘娘’，她也要捂住脸回答：‘走开，你这颗鸵鸟蛋！’因为她认不得他了。明天我们剥下他这身好衣裳，给咱们谢赫穿上；给阿卜杜拉换上一身破烂，烂得连洪水退时奴隶鞋上的泥都不配擦。再把他双手捆在腋下，套上笼头，牵着满城走。他要向百姓喊冤，说自己是阿卜杜拉，我们也齐声回答：‘瞧这疯子，安拉没给他长胡子，他倒自认是贝都因人的谢赫！里雅得的众人啊，听这头驴一叫，你们就知道春天来了。’”

“如今我算明白了，驴叫里头也兴许藏着智慧，”乞丐们的谢赫说，“虽然巴兰·伊本·比珥（Balaam ibn Beor）捂着耳朵不肯听，还因咒骂受了罚，舌头耷拉到胸口，一辈子像条渴狗的舌头。这是好主意，这样我们既不见那人的血，也不担害命的罪名；而且我想哈立德必重重赏我们，他的王国也在笑声里得救了。”

这半天里阿卜杜拉一动没动，他知道自己落在众多仇敌手里，求助无门；可听到乞丐谢赫的裁决，他羞得无地自容，在地板上滚来滚去，像要挣脱捆绑似的。阿尔玛斯塔倒安静地躺在放她的地方，她眼见成功已成泡影，正盘算着怎样利用眼下的处境救自己。

===Original===
Then the beggars laid hold of Abdullah and held him, while others took the sack from his head. He was indeed half smothered with dust, so that at first he could not speak aloud, but coughed and sneezed like a dog that has thrust its nose into a dust-heap to find the bone which is hidden underneath. But presently he recovered his breath and began to rail at them and curse them. To this they paid no attention, but brought the oil lamp near him, and one began to rub soap upon his face and head while another got the razor with which the beggars shaved their heads and began to whet it upon his leathern girdle.

“Do not waste the precious stones of your eloquence upon a barber,” said the Sheikh of the beggars, “but reserve your breath and the rich treasures of your speech until you are brought as a plucked bird before the people of Riad. Moreover we only wish to shave off your beard, but if you are restless some of your hide will certainly be removed also, whereby you will be hurt and it will be still harder for your friends to recognise you tomorrow. It is also useless to shout and scream as though you were driving camels, for you are in the cellar of my house which is at a good distance from other habitations, on the borders of the city.”

So Abdullah saw that there was no escape, and that his fate was about his neck, and he sat still as they had placed him, while the one-eyed hunchback shaved off his beard and the hair on his upper lip and his eyebrows, and the lock at the back of his head.

When this was done the blind man put out his hand and felt Abdullah’s face.

===Chinese===
于是乞丐们捉住阿卜杜拉按住，另一些人把他头上的口袋摘了。他果然叫灰呛得半死，起初高声不得，只是咳嗽打喷嚏，像条把鼻子扎进灰堆里刨底下藏的骨头的狗。可不多时他喘过气来，便开口大骂，咒他们。众人理也不理，只把油灯端近，一个往他脸上头上抹胰子，另一个取来乞丐们剃头用的剃刀，在皮腰带上蹭了起来。

“别把您口才的宝石浪费在剃头匠身上，”乞丐们的谢赫说，“留着您的气力和言语的宝库，等您像只拔了毛的鸟被牵到里雅得众人面前时再用。况且我们不过想剃掉您的胡子，您若不老实，难免连皮也刮下一块来，那时您受了伤，明天您的朋友们更难认出您了。喊叫也没用，赶骆驼也不用这样喊，您这是在我家的地窖里，离人家住处远着呢，就在城边上。”

阿卜杜拉见逃是逃不掉了，命就悬在脖子上，便按他们摆的样子坐定不动，任独眼驼背剃去他的胡子和唇上的髭、眉毛，还有后脑那一绺头发。

剃完，瞎子伸出手去，摸了摸阿卜杜拉的脸。

===Original===
“Surely,” he said, “this is not a man’s head, but the round end of a walking-staff, rubbed smooth by much use.”

They also tied his hands under his armpits and put upon him a ragged shirt with sleeves so that he seemed to have lost both arms at the elbow.

“This is very well done,” said the hunchback turning his head from side to side in order to see all with his one eye. “But what shall we do with the woman? Let us cast lots for her, and he who wins her shall marry her, and we will hold the feast immediately, for we have not yet supped and there is some of the camel’s meat which we received today at the palace.”

“O my brothers,” answered the Sheikh of the beggars, “let us do nothing unlawful in our haste. For this woman is certainly one of Abdullah’s wives, as you may see by her clothes, and unless he divorces her none of us can take her for ourselves, seeing that she is the wife of a believer. Take the sack from her head, however, and if she deafens us with her screaming we can put it on again. But you must by no means put her to shame by taking the veil from her face, for she may be an honest wife, though her husband be a dog. If she has done well, we shall find it out, and no harm will have come to her; but if she is a sharer in this fellow’s plans, her punishment will be grievous, since she will be the wife of an outcast, having neither beard nor eyebrows and rejected by all men.”

===Chinese===
“说真的，”他说，“这哪里是人的脑袋，分明是手杖那圆头，用得多了，磨得溜光。”

他们又把他双手捆在腋下，给他罩上一件带袖子的破衫子，看上去倒像两条胳膊齐肘都没了。

“办得漂亮，”驼背说着左右转脑袋，好用那只眼把一切看全，“可这女人怎么办？咱们抓阄吧，谁抓着就娶她，喜宴马上就摆，咱们晚饭还没吃，今天宫里赏的骆驼肉还剩着些呢。”

“我的兄弟们，”乞丐们的谢赫答道，“再急也不能做犯法的事。这女人瞧衣裳便知定是阿卜杜拉的妻妾之一，他一日不休她，咱们谁也娶不得，因为她是信士之妻。不过可以把她头上的口袋取下来，她若叫唤得震聋我们的耳朵，再套上就是。只是千万不可揭她的面纱叫她受辱，她丈夫纵然是条狗，她或许是本分妇人。她若清白，自会查明，她也不吃亏；她若与这厮同谋，那惩罚就重了，她要做被众人唾弃、没胡子没眉毛的弃徒之妻。”

===Original===
Some of the beggars murmured at this, but most of them praised their Sheikh’s wisdom, and would indeed have feared greatly to break the holy law, being chiefly devout men who prayed daily in the mosque and listened to the Khotbah on Friday. They therefore placed Almasta in one corner of the cellar and Abdullah in another, so that the two could not converse together, and then they took out such food as they had and began to eat their supper, laughing and talking over the jest and anticipating the reward which awaited them for saving Khaled.

In the meanwhile the night was advancing and many of Abdullah’s friends left their houses secretly and gathered in the neighbourhood of the palace to wait for the first signal from within. By threes and by twos and singly they came out of their dwellings, looking to the right and left to see whether they were not the first, as men do who are not sure of being in the right. All had their swords with them, and some their bows also, and some few carried their spears, and they made no secret of their bearing weapons; but under each man’s aba was concealed the largest barley-sack he could find in his house, and concerning this no one of the multitude said anything to his neighbour, for each hoped to get a greater share than the others of the gold and precious stones from the fabulous treasure stored in the palace. Then most of these men sat down to wait, as vultures do before the camel is quite dead. But not long after the middle of the night they were joined by a great throng of Bedouins from Abdullah’s tribe. These had been admitted into the city by the watchman according to the agreement, and passed up the great street from the Hasa gate, in a close body, not speaking and making but little noise with their feet as they walked; yet all of them together could be heard from a distance, because they were so many, and the sound was like the night wind among the branches of dry palm trees. After them, other Bedouins came in from camps both near and far, some of them having made half a day’s journey since sunset; and they surrounded the palace on all sides, and filled the great street, and the street which passes by the mosque towards the Dereyiyah gate and all the other approaches to the open square, sitting down wherever there was room, or leaning against the closed shops of the bazaar, or standing up in a thick crowd when they were too closely pressed to be at ease. They talked together from time to time in low tones, but when their voices rose above a whisper some man in authority hushed them saying that the hour was not yet come.

“By this time Abdullah has slain Khaled,” said some, “and the daughter of the old Sultan is a prisoner.”

===Chinese===
有几个乞丐嘀咕不满，多数却夸谢赫明智，他们到底怕犯圣法——这些人多半虔诚，天天上清真寺礼拜，周五听呼图白（Khotbah）讲道。于是他们把阿尔玛斯塔安置在地窖一角，阿卜杜拉安置在另一角，叫两人说不上话，然后取出所有的吃食用起晚饭来，一面笑谈这桩玩笑，一面指望救了哈立德之后等着他们的赏赐。

这时夜越来越深，阿卜杜拉的党羽们有许多悄悄出了门，聚到王宫左近，等宫里的头一个信号。他们三人一伙、两人一对、也有单身的，出了家门，左右张望，看自己是不是头一个到——心里没底的人都是这般模样。人人带着剑，有的还带弓，少数扛着长矛，带着兵器也毫不遮掩；只是每人的阿巴斗篷底下都藏着家里能找见的最大的大麦口袋，这层谁也不对旁人说破，因为人人都盼着从宫里那传说般的宝库中分得比旁人更多的金子和宝石。随后这些人多半坐下来等，像兀鹰等着骆驼断气。可午夜过后不久，阿卜杜拉部落的大批贝都因人来同他们会合。他们按约定叫看门人放进城，从哈萨门顺着大街上来，队伍紧簇，不出声，脚步也放得极轻；只是人多，远远仍听得见，那声响像夜风刮过干椰枣树的枝杈。在他们之后，别的贝都因人也从远近各处的营地陆续进城，有的自日落走了半日的路；他们把王宫四面围住，挤满大街，挤满经清真寺通往德拉伊耶门的那条街和通向广场的各条路口，有地方便坐下，或倚着集市关了板的铺面，挤得难受时便密密匝匝站起来。他们不时低声交谈，声音一高过耳语，便有管事的人喝住，说时辰未到。

“这会儿阿卜杜拉已经杀了哈立德，”有的说，“老苏丹的女儿成了俘虏。”

===Original===
“And by this time,” said others, “Abdullah is surely unlocking the treasure chamber and filling a barley-sack with pearls and rubies. It is certain that he who slays the lion deserves his bride, but we hope that something will be left for us.”

“Hush!” said the voice of one moving in the darkness. “Be patient. It is not yet time.”

Then, for a space, a deep silence fell on the speakers and they crouched in their places watching the high black walls of the palace and marking the motion of the stars by the highest point of the tower. Before long whispered words were heard again.

“It would have been more just if Abdullah had opened the gate to us as soon as he had slain Khaled, for then we could have seen what he took. But now, who shall tell us what share of the riches he is hiding away in the more secret vaults?”

===Chinese===
“这会儿，”另一些人说，“阿卜杜拉准在开宝库的门，往大麦口袋里装珍珠红宝石。杀了狮子的人配得新娘，这没得说，可总指望能给我们剩点什么。”

“嘘！”黑暗里有个人声在动，“忍着。时辰还没到。”

一时间众人静默下来，伏在原处，望着王宫高耸的黑墙，以塔尖为界观察星辰的移转。不多时，又起了窃窃私语。

“阿卜杜拉杀了哈立德就该立刻开门放我们进去，那才公道，我们也好看他拿了什么。如今呢，他把财宝藏进更隐秘的地窖里多少，谁来告诉我们？”

===Original===
“This is true,” answered others. “And besides, what need have we of Abdullah to help us into the palace? Surely we could have broken down the gates and slain the guards and Khaled himself without Abdullah’s help. Yet we, for our part, would not shed the blood of a man who has always dealt very generously with us, nor do we believe the story of the camels laden secretly in Haïl. However, what is ordained will take place, and we shall undoubtedly receive plentiful gold merely for sitting here to watch the stars through the night.”

“The story of the camels is not true,” said a certain man, speaking alone. “For I was of the drivers sent with them, and being hungry, we opened one of the bales on the way. By Allah! There was nothing but wheat in it, and it was white and good; but there was nothing else, not so much as a few small coins﻿—”

Then there was the sound of a blow, and the man who was speaking was struck on the mouth, so that his speech was interrupted.

“Peace and be silent!” said a voice. “They who speak lies will receive no share with the rest when the time comes.”

===Chinese===
“这话不假，”另一些人应道，“再说，进王宫何用阿卜杜拉帮忙？没有他，我们照样砸得开大门，杀得了卫兵，也杀得了哈立德本人。只是我们自己不愿见一个向来待我们极为慷慨之人的血，也不信哈伊勒那批骆驼暗中驮宝的传闻。罢了，命里注定的总要发生，我们坐在这儿看一宿星星，就白白得一大笔金子。”

“骆驼的事不是真的，”有一个人独自开口，“我就是赶那趟骆驼的，路上饿了，解开了一包货。凭安拉！里面除了麦子什么都没有，麦子又白又好；别的什么也没有，连几个小钱都没有﻿——”

随即响起一记耳光，说话的人嘴上挨了一下，话被打断了。

“安分些，闭嘴！”一个声音说，“说谎的人，到时候休想同大伙一起分。”

===Original===
But the man who had been struck was the strongest of all his tribe, though he who had struck him did not know it. And the man caught his assailant by the waist in the dark, and wrestled with him violently, being very angry, and broke his forearm and his collarbone and several of his ribs, and when he had done with him, he threw him over his shoulder so that he fell fainting and moaning three paces away.

“O you who strike honest men on the mouth in the dark, you have been over-rash!” he cried. “Go home and hide yourself lest I recognise you and break such bones as you have still whole!”

“This is well done,” said one of the bystanders in a loud voice. “For the story of the camels laden secretly with treasure is a lie. I also was with the drivers and ate of the wheat. Nor do I believe that Khaled is a robber and a Persian.”

“We do not believe it!” cried a score of Bedouins together. “And if we have come here, it is to get our share like other men, since they tell us that Khaled is dead. But now we believe that Abdullah has shut himself into the palace and means to keep all for himself, and is cheating us.”

===Chinese===
可挨打的人是全族力气最大的，打他的人并不知道。那人在黑暗里拦腰抓住打他的，怒不可遏，同他狠命相搏，折断了他的前臂和锁骨，还断了几根肋骨，收拾完了，把他从肩上甩出去，摔在三步开外，昏过去直哼哼。

“你这黑地里打老实人嘴巴的，你可太莽撞了！”他喊道，“回家躲起来吧，省得叫我认出来，把你还没断的骨头也敲断！”

“打得好！”旁边有人大声说，“骆驼暗驮财宝的事本是谣言。我也跟过那趟驼队，吃过那麦子。我也不信哈立德是强盗、是波斯人。”

“我们也不信！”二十来个贝都因人齐声喊，“我们来这儿，是听说哈立德死了，想像旁人一样分我们的一份。可如今我们倒信，阿卜杜拉把自己关进了王宫，想一个人独吞，在骗我们。”

===Original===
These men were none of them of Abdullah’s tribe, but as the voices grew louder, Abdullah’s kinsmen came up, and endeavoured to quiet the growing tumult. The crowd had parted a little and the strong man stood alone in the midst.

“We pray you to be patient,” said Abdullah’s men, “for the time is at hand and the false dawn has already passed, though you have not seen it, so that before long it will be day. Then the gates will be opened and you shall all go in.”

“We have no need of your sheikh to open gates for us,” said the strong man, in a voice that could be heard very far through the crowd. “And moreover it will be better for you not to strike any more of us, or, by Allah, we will not only break your bones but shed your blood.”

At this there was a sullen cry and men sprang to their feet and laid their hands upon their weapons. But a youth who had come up with Abdullah’s kinsmen, though not one of them, bent very low over the man who had been thrown down and then spoke out with a loud and laughing voice.

===Chinese===
这些人没有一个属于阿卜杜拉的部落。人声越来越响，阿卜杜拉的族人便围了上来，竭力平息越闹越大的乱子。人群稍稍闪开，那大力士独自站在当中。

“请诸位忍耐，”阿卜杜拉的人说，“时候就在眼前，假晓已经过去了，只是你们没看见，天很快就要亮。那时自会开门，你们都进得去。”

“用不着你们的谢赫替我们开门，”大力士说，声音穿过人群传得很远，“还有，你们最好别再动手打我们的人，不然凭安拉起誓，我们不但敲断你们的骨头，还要你们的命。”

这话激起一片含怒的喊声，人们纷纷跳起来，手按兵器。有个小伙子随阿卜杜拉的族人同来，却不是他们一族，他朝那个被摔在地的人深深俯下身去，随后放声笑着开了口。

===Original===
“Truly they say that crows lead people to the carcases of dogs!” he said. “This fellow is of the family which murdered my father, upon whom may Allah send peace! Nor will I exceed the bounds of moderation and justice.”

Thereupon the young man drew out his knife and immediately killed his father’s enemy as he lay upon the ground, and then he withdrew quickly into the dark crowd so that none knew him. But though there was only the light of the stars and the multitude was great, many had seen the deed and each man stood closer by his neighbour and grasped his weapon to be in readiness. The kinsmen of Abdullah saw that they were separated from their own tribe and drew back, warning the others to keep the peace and be silent, lest they should be cut off from their share of the spoil. But their voices trembled with fears for their own safety, and they were answered by scornful shouts and jeers.

“The young man says well that you are crows,” cried the angry men, “for you wish to keep the carcase for yourselves. Come and take it if you are able!”

===Chinese===
“人说得真不假，乌鸦引着人去找死狗！”他说，“这家伙正是害死我父亲的那家人——愿安拉赐我父亲平安！我也不会越出节制和公道的界限。”

说罢，小伙子拔出刀来，就地结果了那个躺在地上的杀父仇人，随即飞快退进黑暗的人群，谁也没认出他来。虽然只有星光，人又极多，许多人还是看见了这一幕，人人都往同伴身边靠紧，攥住兵器以防不测。阿卜杜拉的族人见自己同本部落的人隔开了，便往后退，一面警告众人守静别作声，免得分不到赃。可他们的声音因惧怕自身安危而发颤，回答他们的是一片嘲骂和讥笑。

“小伙子说你们是乌鸦，说得对！”愤怒的人们喊道，“你们想把死尸独吞。有本事就来拿！”

===Original===
Now indeed the quarrel which had been begun by the blow struck in the dark spread suddenly to great dimensions, for the words spoken were caught up as grains of sand by the wind and blown into all men’s ears. Many were ready enough to believe that Abdullah cared only for enriching himself and his tribe, and many more who had been persuaded to the enterprise by the hope of gain turned again to their faith in Khaled as the dream of gold disappeared from their eyes. Yet Abdullah’s tribe was numerous, and it was easy to see that if the dissension grew into a strife of arms the fight would be long and fierce on both sides.

Then certain of those who were against Abdullah raised the cry that he had slain Khaled and escaped with the treasure by a secret passage leading under the walls of the city, which passage was spoken of in old tales, though no one knew where to find it. But the multitude believed and pressed forward in a strong body and began to beat against the iron-bound gate of the palace with great stones and pieces of wood. Abdullah’s men came on fiercely to prevent them, but were opposed by many, and as the wing of night was lifted and the dawn drank the stars, the wide square was filled with the clashing of arms and the noise of a terrible tumult.

===Chinese===
这场由黑暗中一记耳光挑起的争端，果然霎时间闹大了，那些话像风卷沙粒，吹进了每个人的耳朵。许多人本就乐意相信阿卜杜拉只顾自家和本部落发财，更多原为财而来的人，眼见黄金梦散了，又回心转意信起哈立德来。只是阿卜杜拉的部落人多势众，一眼便看得出：这场龃龉若动起刀兵，两边必有一场恶战、久战。

于是反阿卜杜拉的人里有几个高声喊起来，说他杀了哈立德，带着财宝从城墙下一条秘道跑了——那秘道古书里提过，可谁也不知道在哪里。众人竟信了，结成一大股往前涌，搬起大石块和木料，砸起王宫包铁的大门来。阿卜杜拉的人凶狠地冲上来阻拦，却遭众人抵抗。这时夜幕掀起，黎明饮尽了星辰，宽阔的广场上响彻兵刃相击之声和可怕的骚乱。

## XII / 十二

===Original===
At the time when the beggars were carrying away Abdullah and his wife, Khaled was sitting in his accustomed place, silent and heavy at heart, and Zehowah played softly to him upon a *barbat* and sang a sad song in a low voice. For she saw that gloominess had overcome him and she feared to disturb his mood, though she would gladly have made him smile if she had been able.

A black slave of Khaled’s whom he had treated with great kindness had secretly told him that there was a plan to enter the palace with evil during that night, for the fellow had spied upon those who knew and had overheard what he now told his master. He had also asked whether he should not warn the guards of the palace, in order that a strict watch should be kept, but Khaled had bidden him be silent.

“Either the guards are conspiring with the rest,” said Khaled, “and will be the first to attack me, or they are ignorant of the plan; and if so how can they withstand so great a multitude? I will abide by my own fate, and no man shall lose his life for my sake unless he desires to do so.”

But he privately put on a coat of mail under his aba, and when he sat down in the harem to await the end he would not let Zehowah take his sword, but laid it upon his feet and sat upright against the wall, looking towards the door.

===Chinese===
乞丐们把阿卜杜拉夫妇俩抬走的时候，哈立德正坐在他惯常的座位上，默然无语，心头沉重，泽霍瓦轻轻为他拨着巴尔巴特琴，低声唱一支忧伤的歌。她看出愁绪已经罩住了他，不敢搅扰他的心境，虽然若做得到，她原是很愿博他一笑的。

哈立德手下一个受过他大恩的黑奴，已经悄悄告诉他，今夜有人要行凶闯宫，因为这家伙暗地里盯着那些知情的人，把他如今禀报主人的话偷听了个着实。黑奴还问，要不要知会宫里的卫兵，好严加提防，哈立德却吩咐他不要作声。

“卫兵要么同他们是一伙的，”哈立德说，“会抢先动手杀我；要么就是对此事一无所知——若是一无所知，他们又怎挡得住这么大队的人马？我认我自己的命，谁也不许为我送命，除非他自己情愿。”

可他悄悄在阿巴斗篷底下穿上了锁子甲，坐进内宫等候结局的时候，他不肯让泽霍瓦把剑拿去，而是把它横放在脚上，背挺得笔直，靠着墙坐定，眼睛望着门口。

===Original===
“Since I have no soul,” he said to himself, “this is probably the end of all things. But there is no reason why I should not kill as many of these murderers as possible.”

He was gloomy and desponding, however, since he saw that his hour was at hand, and that Zehowah was no nearer to loving him than before. He watched her fingers as she played upon the instrument, and he listened to the soft notes of her voice.

“It is a strange thing,” he thought, “and I believe that she is not able to love, any more than my sword upon my feet, which is good and true and beautiful, and ever ready to my hand, but is itself cold, having no feeling in it.”

Still Zehowah sang and Khaled heard her song, listening watchfully for a man’s tread upon the threshold and looking to see a man’s face and the light of steel in the shadow beyond the lamps.

===Chinese===
“我既然没有灵魂，”他自忖道，“这多半就是万事的尽头了。可多杀几个这帮凶手，总没有什么不该。”

然而他忧郁消沉，因为眼看自己的时辰已到，而泽霍瓦对他的爱并不比先前更近一分。他望着她抚琴的手指，听她嗓音里柔和的音调。

“真是件怪事，”他想，“我看她根本不会爱，就像我脚上这把剑，它又好又真又美，随手可取，它自己却是冷的，里头没有知觉。”

泽霍瓦还在唱，哈立德听着歌，一面警醒地留意门槛上有没有男人的脚步，留意灯火照不到的阴影里会不会现出一张男人的脸和钢铁的寒光。

===Original===
“The night is long,” he said at last, aloud.

“It is not yet midnight,” Zehowah answered. “But you are tired. Will you not go to rest?”

“I shall rest tomorrow,” said Khaled. “Tonight I will sit here and look at you, if you will sing to me.”

Zehowah gazed into his eyes, wondering a little at his exceeding sadness. Then she bowed her head and struck the strings of the instrument to a new measure more melancholy than the last, and sang an old song of many verses, with a weeping refrain.

===Chinese===
“夜真长啊。”他终于出声说道。

“还不到半夜呢，”泽霍瓦答道，“可你累了。不去歇息吗？”

“我明天再歇，”哈立德说，“今夜我要坐在这儿看你，只要你肯唱歌给我听。”

泽霍瓦凝视他的眼睛，有些诧异他何以忧伤到这般地步。随后她低下头，换了一支比先前更凄凉的调子拨响琴弦，唱起一首许多段的老歌，副歌如泣如诉。

===Original===
“Are you also heavy at heart tonight?” Khaled asked, when he had listened to the end.

“It is not easy to kindle a lamp when the rain is falling heavily,” Zehowah said. “Your sadness has taken hold of me, like the chill of a fever. I cannot laugh tonight.”

“And yet you have a good cause, for they say that tonight the Earth is to be delivered of a great malefactor, a certain Persian, whose name is perhaps Hassan, a notorious robber.”

Khaled turned away his head, smiling bitterly, for he desired not to see the satisfaction which would come into her face.

===Chinese===
“今夜你心里也沉重么？”哈立德听完，问道。

“雨下得紧的时候，灯是不容易点亮的，”泽霍瓦说，“你的忧伤缠上了我，像热病的寒战。今夜我笑不出来。”

“可你有个好由头，人家说，今夜大地就要摆脱一个大恶棍了，一个波斯人，名字多半叫哈桑，一个臭名昭著的强盗。”

哈立德扭过头去，苦笑着，因为他不愿看见她脸上将浮起的快意。

===Original===
“This is a poor jest,” she answered in a low voice, and the *barbat* rolled from her knees to the carpet beside her.

“I mean no jesting, for I do not desire to disappoint you, since you will naturally be glad to be freed from me. But I am glad if you are willing to sing to me, for this night is very long.”

“Do you think that I believe this of you?” asked Zehowah, after some time.

“You believed it yesterday, you believe it today, and you will believe it tomorrow when you are free to make choice of some other man﻿—whom you will doubtless love.”

===Chinese===
“这个玩笑开得可怜，”她低声答道，巴尔巴特琴从她膝上滚落，掉在身旁的地毯上。

“我不是说笑，我不想叫你失望，摆脱了我，你自然要高兴的。不过你若肯唱歌给我听，我是欢喜的，这夜实在太长了。”

“你以为我会信你这话么？”过了半晌，泽霍瓦问道。

“你昨天信，今天信，等你自由了，另拣了一个男人﻿——你无疑会爱的那个男人——明天你还是会信。”

===Original===
“Yet I know that it is not true,” she said suddenly.

“It is too late,” Khaled answered. “The more I love you, the more I see how little faith you have in me﻿—and the less faith can I put in you. Will you sing to me again?”

“This is very cruel and bitter.” Zehowah sighed and looked at him.

“Will you sing to me again, Zehowah?” he repeated. “I like your sad music.”

===Chinese===
“可我知道这不是真的。”她忽然说。

“太迟了，”哈立德答道，“我越爱你，越看清你对我何等少信﻿——我也越难信你。再为我唱一支好么？”

“这话说得太狠、太苦了。”泽霍瓦叹了口气，望着他。

“再为我唱一支好么，泽霍瓦？”他又说了一遍，“我喜欢你忧伤的曲子。”

===Original===
Then she took up the *barbat* from the carpet, but though she struck a chord she could not go on and her hand lay idle upon the strings, and her voice was still.

“You are perhaps tired,” said Khaled after some time. “Then lay aside the instrument and sleep.” He composed himself in his seat, his sword being ready and his eyes towards the door.

But Zehowah shook her head as though awaking from a dream, her fingers ran swiftly over the strings and gentle tones came from her lips. Khaled listened thoughtfully to the song and the words soothed him, but before she had reached the end, she stopped suddenly.

“Why do you not finish it?” he asked.

===Chinese===
她于是从地毯上拾起巴尔巴特琴，可弦虽拨响了一声，却弹不下去，手闲搁在弦上，嗓音也哑然了。

“你许是累了，”过了半晌，哈立德说，“那就放下琴，睡去吧。”他在座位上安顿好身子，剑放在手边，眼睛望着门口。

泽霍瓦却摇了摇头，像从梦中醒来，手指飞快地在弦上跑动，唇间流出温柔的音调。哈立德若有所思地听着歌，那歌词安抚了他，可她还没唱到结尾，忽然停住了。

“怎么不唱完？”他问。

===Original===
“If you have told me truth,” she answered, “this is no time for singing and music. But if not, why should I labour to amuse you, as though I were a slave? I will call one of the women who has a sweet voice and a good memory. She will sing you a *kasid* which will last till morning.”

“You are wrong,” said Khaled. “There is no reason in what you say.”

But he reflected upon her nature, while he spoke.

“Surely,” he thought, “there is nothing in the world so contradictory as a woman. I ask of her a song and she is silent. I bid her rest, supposing her to be weary, and she sings to me. If I tell her that I hate her she will perhaps answer that she loves me. *Min Allah!* Let us see.”

===Chinese===
“你若对我说了实话，”她答道，“眼下就不是唱歌奏乐的时候。若没有，我又何必费力取悦你，倒像个奴隶似的？我去唤一个嗓音甜、记性好的女人来，让她给你唱一支卡西达（*kasid*），能一直唱到天亮。”

“你错了，”哈立德说，“你这话毫无道理。”

嘴上这么说着，他心里却琢磨着她的性子。

“真的，”他想，“世上再没有比女人更自相矛盾的东西了。我向她讨一支歌，她偏沉默；我当她累了叫她歇息，她偏唱歌给我听。我若对她说我恨她，她多半要答说她爱我。*Min Allah*！倒要看看。”

===Original===
“You inspire hatred in me,” he said aloud, after a few moments.

At this Zehowah was very much astonished, and she again let the *barbat* fall from her knees.

“You wished me to believe that you loved me, and this not long since,” she answered.

“It may be so. I did not know you then.”

===Chinese===
过了片刻，他出声说道：“你激起我的恨了。”

这话叫泽霍瓦大吃一惊，巴尔巴特琴又从她膝上滑落了。

“不久之前，你还叫我相信你是爱我的。”她答道。

“也许是吧。那时我还不认识你。”

===Original===
He looked towards the door as though he would say nothing further. Zehowah sighed, not understanding him yet being wounded in that sensitive tissue of the heart which divides the outer desert of pride from the inner garden of love, belonging to neither but separating the two as a veil. And when there is a rent in that veil, pride looks on love and scoffs bitterly, and love looks on pride and weeps tears of fire.

“I am sorry that you hate me,” she said, but the words were bitter in her mouth as a draught from a spring into which the enemy have cast wormwood, that none may drink of it.

“Allah is great!” thought Khaled. “This is already an advantage.”

Then Zehowah took up the *barbat* and began to sing a careless song not like any which Khaled had ever heard. This is the song﻿—

===Chinese===
他朝门口望去，仿佛再没有什么可说的了。泽霍瓦叹了口气，她不懂他，却在心口那层娇嫩的薄膜上受了伤——那膜隔开外面骄傲的荒漠和里面爱的园圃，两不相属，却像一面帷幕把两者分开。帷幕一旦裂开一道口子，骄傲便俯视着爱，狠狠讥诮；爱便仰望着骄傲，流下火一样的泪。

“你恨我，我很难过。”她说，可这话在她口中苦涩难当，像一眼被仇人投了苦艾的泉水，从此叫谁也无法再饮。

“安拉是伟大的！”哈立德想道，“这已经是一步进展了。”

泽霍瓦重新拿起巴尔巴特琴，唱起一支散漫无羁的歌，哈立德从未听过这样的调子。歌是这样唱的﻿——

===Original===
> “The fisherman of Oman tied the halter under his arms,
 

 The sky was as blue as the sea in winter.
 

 The fisherman dived into the deep waters
 

 As a ray of light shoots through a sapphire of price.
 

 The sea was as blue as the sky, for it was winter.
 

 Among the rocks below the water it was dark and cold
 

 Though the sky above was as blue as a fine sapphire.
 

 The fisherman saw a rough shell lying there in the dark between two crabs,
 

 ‘In that shell there must be a large pearl,’ he said.
 

 But when he would have taken it the crabs ran together and fastened upon his hand.
 

 His heart was bursting in his ribs for lack of breath
 

 And he thought of the sky above, as blue as the sea in winter.
 

 So he pulled the halter and was taken half-fainting into the boat.
 

 The crabs held his hand but he struck them off,
 

 And his heart beat merrily as he breathed the wind
 

 Blowing over the sea as blue as the sky in winter.
 

 ‘There are no pearls in this ocean,’ he said to his companions,
 

 ‘But there are crabs if anyone cares to dive.’
 

 One of them saw the shell caught between the legs of the crabs,
 

 He opened it and found a pearl of the value of a kingdom.
